營收走勢圖

研究員/陳晏平 TINA.YP.CHEN@uni-psg.com

1H26 主要認列案源為中和 K Tower Y09 商辦案、中和心禾匯住宅
案、台北信義豪宅案持續去化與遞延交屋，1H26 累積營收為 132 億
元 ， 成長31%YOY， 且在整體毛利率較2025 年同期些微提升下 ，1H26
累積稅後淨利為 11.1 億元，成長 61%YOY，EPS 為 1.87 元。展望
2H26，兩個台中新完工建案(合計總銷金額 83 億元)遞延至 4Q26 才
正式推出銷售，且台中市地區為 2026 年以來銷售速度較慢的區域，
預期此兩個台中建案在 2026 年認列案量較少，主要將貢獻在 2027
年。所幸，近幾個月財富效果開始顯現，進而有提升居住品質、資
產配置需求，台北市信義豪宅案 1H26 已認列逾 10 億元，銷售優於
預期 。 因此 ， 研究部預估2026 年合併營收為 268 億元 ，成長 17%YOY，
稅後淨利 20 億元，成長 27%YOY，EPS 為 3.24 元，獲利將自谷底回
升；並預估 2027 年合併營收為 301 億元，成長 12%YOY，稅後淨利
為 24 億元，成長 21%YOY，EPS 為 3.92 元，營運將呈現穩增。投資
建議方面，雖目前房市相關政策已不再緊縮，並朝向逐步鬆綁，加
上高科技產業景氣成長強勁、股市上漲帶來財富效果，使得房市氣
氛將逐漸走出谷底，但因研究部預期冠德 2026~2027 年獲利皆將落
在 3~4 元之譜，未明顯跳增，近兩年現金殖利率亦約當 4~5%，未明
顯高於同業，故維持給予0.5xNAV，目標價40 元，潛在上漲空間有
限，重申中立建議。

重點摘要： 
1. 1H26認列案源主要為帳上成屋持續去化，累積稅後 EPS為1.87
元： 
受惠:(1) 中和K Tower Y09( 原中和秀朗橋捷運聯開商辦案，
總銷金額31億元，銷售率30%)新完工建案開始交屋；(2) 中和
心禾匯(原中和LG08捷運聯開住宅案，總銷金額16億元，銷售
率80%)、 台北市冠德信義豪宅案持續去化與遞延交屋入帳 ；(3)
轉投資根基營造合併營收貢獻大增75%QOQ、52%YOY至61億元，
進而帶動2Q26單季合併 營收明顯成長72%QOQ、57%YOY至84億
元。另在營造子公司有個案結算以及高毛利率冠德信義豪宅案
認列較多下 ， 毛利率亦有所提升，2Q26單季毛利率上揚至28%，
季增4個百分點、年增5個百分點，再扣除費用、所得稅負、根
基少數股權影響，2Q26單季稅後淨利為8億元(歸屬母公司)，
跳增160%QOQ、224%YOY，單季EPS為1.35元。

合計1H26累積營收為132億元，成長31%YOY，且在整體毛利率
較2025年同期些微提升下，1H26累積稅後淨利為11.1億元，成
長61%YOY，EPS為1.87元。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

2. 兩個台中新完工建案遞延至4Q26才正式推出銷售， 預估2026年
稅後EPS將自谷底回升至3.24元： 
展望2H26，除帳上成屋案持續去化外，冠德 規劃10~11月將陸
續推出台中裕毛屋住宅案(總銷金額67億元)、 台中G5捷運聯開
商辦案(總銷金額16億元)、台北市萬華直興住宅 案(總銷金額
36億元)，合計新推出案量共119億元。其中，台中裕毛屋住宅
案已於2026年6月取得使照，並有先行潛銷，預計2026年10月
正式推出銷售 ；台中G5捷運聯開商辦案亦已取得使照，計畫
2026年11月開始銷售 ，此兩案皆 將視銷售成績在2H26交屋入
帳。

考量:(1) 台中為2026年以來房屋銷售較慢的區域，且台中裕
毛屋住宅案、台中G5捷運聯開商辦案皆延至4Q26才開始銷售，
預期2026年認列比例將較原先預期少，推估將分別認列 20%、
10%案量；(2) 中和心禾匯、中和K Tower Y09可於2026年順利
銷售並完成交屋25%、1/3案量；(3) 財富效果顯現，進而有提
升居住品質、資產配置需求，預期台北市冠德信義豪宅案可去
化20億元金額，較2025年認列金額倍增。再加計營造、百貨事
業營收貢獻 ， 預估2026年合併營收為268億元 ，成長17%YOY，稅
後淨利20億元，成長27%YOY，EPS為3.24元，將自谷底回升。

3. 2027年將有一台北市住宅案完工 ，加計帳上成屋去化， 預估
2027年稅後EPS將穩增至3.92元： 
展望2027年，除持續銷售前述個案外，預期4Q27還將新推出南
港台電公辦都更住宅案，該案總銷金額達130億元，將視銷售
情況於2028年完工之時認列。而在建案認列方面，預期將有總
銷金額36億元的台北市萬華直興住宅案於3Q27完工 ， 研究部採
保守原則，推估此案在2027年將可順利去化並認列30%案量，
加計:(1)中和心禾匯、中和K Tower Y09、台中裕毛屋住宅案、
台中G5捷運聯開商辦案 ， 推估將分別去化 、 認列15%、1/3、60%、
60%案量 ；(2)台北市冠德信義豪宅案遞延交屋與持續去化20億
元；(3) 營造 、 百貨事業營收貢獻， 預估2027年合併營收為301
億元，成長12%YOY，稅後淨利為24億元，成長21%YOY，EPS為
3.92元，營運將呈現穩增。

4. 帳上土地庫存充足，估計每股資產價值為77元，目前股價評價
約0.5xNAV： 
在扣除2027年完工建案後，帳上土地庫存仍有2.2萬坪，換算
總銷金額為801億元 ， 未來推案無虞 。 就區域分布上 ， 以台北市
佔比最高，高達62%，其次為高雄市、台中市，佔比各為15%、
14%，另有板橋佔比9%。主要個案包含:板橋官邸公辦都更住宅
案、南港台電公辦都更住宅案、台北市行二行三公辦都更案、

### Page 3

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  3

台中G9-1捷運聯開商辦案、北士科合建住宅案、台北市兒福B2
公辦都更住宅案、板橋Y16捷運聯開商辦案、高雄專四五公辦
都更住商案、台北市通化安和公辦都更住宅案。其中，總銷金
額130億元的南港台電公辦都更住宅案 有望在4Q27推出，若順
銷，將成為2028年業績入帳來源。

除前述在建中建案、土地庫存外，冠德帳上還有2025年新完工
的總部大樓—台北市民權東路商辦案 ，總銷坪數1萬坪，其中
有2000餘坪為冠德自用，為提升商業價值，目前冠德傾向先行
出租，短期暫不會有出售計畫。而目前出租簽約率已達100%，
租客將在2026年陸續進駐 ， 估計一整年可貢獻4億元租金收入 。
整體而言，推估冠德每股資產價值為77元，目前股價位置約當
0.5xNAV。

### Page 4

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  4

5. 預期2027年現金殖利率將在4.4~4.9%區間水準： 
股利政策方面，為保留資本，以提高自有資金比例、降低營運
槓桿，近幾年在正常獲利情況下，現金股利配發率皆落在
35~41%區間水準 。 而因2025年獲利衰退較多 ，2026年配發1.616
元現金股利 ， 且無股票股利 ， 現金股利配發率提升至62%， 已於
2026/8/10除息，除息前現金殖利率4.7%。由於2026年獲利將
自谷底回升，研究部預期2027年現金股利將落在1.6~1.8元區
間水準，換算現金殖利率4.4~4.9%。

附錄 一 ： 評 等 之 標 準 
評等  定義  
強力買進     預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬超過 25%以上  
買進  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬 10% 以上  
中立  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 10～-10%  
降低持股  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 (-10%)以下  
未評等  沒有足夠的基本資料判斷該公司評等

附 錄 二 ： 免 責 宣 言

司 認 可 之 資 料來 源 ， 並 基 於 特 定 日 期 所 做 之 判 斷 ， 但 不 保 證 其 完 整 性 或 正 確 性 ， 報
告 中 所 有 的 意 見 及 預 估 ， 如 有 變 更 恕 不 另 行 通 知 。

供 專 屬 之 投 資 建 議 。 文 中 所 載 資 訊 或 任 何 意 見 ， 不 構 成 任 何 買 賣 有 價 證 券 或 其 他 投

資 標 的 ， 或 文 中 所 討 論 或 建 議 之 投 資 策 略 ， 投 資 人 應 就 其 是 否 適 合 本 身 財 務 狀 況 與
投 資 條 件 ， 進 一 步 諮 詢 財 務 顧 問 的 意 見 。 本 投 資 報 告 之 內 容 取 材 自 據 信 為 可 靠 之 資
料 來 源 ， 但 概 不 以 明 示 或 默 示 的 方 式 ， 對 資 料 之 準 確 性 、 完 整 性 或 正 確 性 作 出 任 何
陳 述 或 保 證 。 本 投 資 報 告 載 述 意 見 進 行 更 改 與 撤 回 並 不 另 行 通 知 。 本 投 資 報 告 並 非
（且不應 解 釋 為 ）在 任 何 司 法 管 轄 區 內 ， 任 何 非 依 法 從 事 證 券 經 紀 或 交 易 之 人 士 或
公 司 ， 為 於 該 管 轄 區 內 從 事 證 券 經 紀 或 交 易 之 遊 說 。 本 投 資 報 告 內 容 屬 統 一 投 顧 之
著 作 權 ， 嚴 禁 抄 襲 與 仿 造 。

Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
In this report, we focus on China's manufacturing PMI, which we
see as a potential catalyst for AirTAC's and Hiwin's share prices.
China's manufacturing PMI edged up  to 50.1 in September from 49.8 in August:
• This came in consistent with both our estimate and consensus of 50.1.
• The production index was up 1.3ppt while the new order index was down

0.1ppt MoM.
• Our macro team believes the upturn was led mainly led by a quarter-end
production push (see their report).
Our view:
• We believe the modest pickup in the  manufacturing PMI should help
sentiment  toward relevant stocks with high exposure to the Chinese market.
Among the automation names, we prefer plays with company-specific drivers
like AirTAC and Hiwin:
° AirTAC (1590.TW, OW) should continue to benefit from its own share
gains and incremental contribution from linear guides. Valuation appears

undemanding at 17x 2027e P/E (vs. the 25x average since 2020).
° Hiwin (2049.TW, OW) should show more margin expansion in coming
quarters, supported by higher utilization and price hikes. We see
potential share price upside at the current 29x 2027e P/E vs. peak cycle
valuation of 35-40x.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
September 30, 2026 05:15 PM GMT

Valuation Methodology and Risks
AirTAC International (1590.TW)
Base case, 29x 2027e P/E. We believe this methodology better captures earnings growth
momentum for industrial automation component plays (market sentiment moves along
with business cycles). As the industry enters an up-cycle, we think applying a near-peak cycle
valuation of 29x P/E is reasonable given the stronger business outlook.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger macroeconomic climate in China

n Stronger-than-expected demand from industrial automation
Risks to Downside
n Sharper economic downturn in China
n Miniature linear guideway business development taking more time
n Larger-than-expected investment in new products but weaker-than-expected demand
Hiwin Technologies Corp. (2049.TW)
Base case, P/E. We think this valuation methodology better captures Hiwin's earnings growth
momentum, as its valuation tends to move with the business cycle. We apply a target multiple

of 38x to our 2027 EPS estimate. We view this multiple as justified by the 47% operating profit
CAGR we project for 2025-28.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger industrial automation demand pickup.
n Market share gains.
n Earlier-than-expected revenue contribution from humanoids.
Risks to Downside
n Weaker industrial automation demand.
n More pricing pressure.
n Market share loss.
n More broad-based impact on the global economy from geopolitical risks.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

## Document 062: 261001_ml_nanyaPCB

### Page 1

NYPCB

Alarm cleared with new drivers into 2027; 
re-it. Buy with PO raise to NT$2,000

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 2,000 TWD | Price: 1,340 TWD

BT substrate price hike resumed from 4Q26 
Following (see report) the worries of moderating BT substrate price hike in early 
September, we now expect NYPCB to still raise relevant ASP by single-digit % in 4Q26, 
and the hike is purely driven by favorable S/D which will fully translate into the accretion 
in bottom-line. Following this, BT substrate price will likely be raised by another 20+% in 
1Q27, due partly to the cost pass-through. We currently bake in ~10% revenue QoQ with 
32-33% GM for 4Q26, but we do not rule out the possibility for its revenue to surprise 
on the upside, should there be a faster ramp of its ABF substrate capacity in Kunshan. 
New driver for 2027: server CPU & mix optimization  
Looking into 2027 we expect the benefits of demand overflow on (see report) server 
CPU side to materialize, and we flag a chance for the firm to record 20+% revenue from 
the emerging business with AMD server CPU, where NYPCB is working on the 
qualification in its Shulin site currently and should capture most of the spillover demand 
if the progress goes smooth. Meanwhile, the firm will likely allocate more capacity from 
other sites (e.g. Kunshan) to fulfill the demand from Broadcom, which also helps enhance 
the mix. Currently we model ~50% GM for its ABF substrate business in 2027, but we 
see chance for further upward revision, driven by more favorable S/D dynamics and mix.  
Raise estimates/PO; prime beneficiary of price hike 
NYPCB’s share price (up ~50%) lags its major peers Unimicron/Kinsus (up ~70%) since 
late-July, but in our view it is still the best proxy to position for price hike in the 
substrate market. Judging from (see report) Kinsus’ August prints last week, we believe 
NYPCB remains on track to meet or beat the market’s expectations on N-T margins, and 
we reiterate Buy on the stock. Along with 13%/18% EPS raise in 2027/28E thanks to the 
more benign pricing/margin outlook driven by further mix improvement and S/D, we 
raise PO to NT$2,000 (from NT$1,700) same based on 35x 2H27-1H28E P/E.

Key Metrics

Company Sector 
Semiconductors

Company Description 
Founded in 1997, NYPCB was listed on the Taiwan Stock 
Exchange in 2006. The firm is a 67%-owned subsidiary of 
Nan Ya Plastics Corporation (1303 TT). NYPCB's main 
products are HDI, PCB and IC substrates (including ABF and 
BT substrates), widely used in telecommunication, 
consumers, PC, and Auto applications. The clients include 
AMD, Broadcom, Samsung, Apple, Continental, etc. The 
main competitors include Unimicron, Kinsus, Ibiden, 
Shinko, and SEMCO, etc.

Investment Rationale 
We have a Buy rating on NYPCB, in view of its continuous 
improvement of utilization rate and gross margin across 
ABF and BT substrate business into 2027, thanks to the 
robust demand growth from fabless customers and 
demand overflow in AI ASICs.

### Page 5

Price objective basis & risk 
NYPCB (NANYF) 
Our PO of NT$2,000 is based on 35x 2H27-1H28E P/E. We view this multiple as fair 
given ROE and operating margin expansion to 39% and 41% in 2027-28E (vs 13% and 
14% in 2025-26E).

Upside risks to our PO are (1) tighter ABF supply, (2) higher SiP/AiP adoption, and (3) 
less intensified competition pressure in PCB business.

Downside risks are (1) greater ABF expansion from peers, (2) muted SiP/AiP adoption, 
and (3) more severe competition in PCB business.

increase in value and are less attractive than Buy rated stocks and 3 - Underperform stocks are the least attractive stocks in a coverage cluster. An investment

## Document 066: 261005_daiwa_TW-CCL

Source PDF: all report pdf/261005_daiwa_TW-CCL.pdf
Report source: 未公開來源
Date: 2026-10-05
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Summary: After market hours on 5 October 2026, EMC, TUC, and ITEQ released their 
monthly sales results. Our read-through is positive as the three key CCL vendors beat 
sales expectations by 5-15% on better pricing and on-going spec upgrades. Our thesis is 
that the strong AI demand is causing a spill-over impact on the whole CCL industry with 
low-end CCL having c.100% price hike in 2026E and high-end CCL seeing a 40-50% price 
hike due to cost inflation in 2026E. Recent data points showed this thesis remains valid. 
Looking ahead, we expect high-end/low-end CCL to see additional c.10%/20% ASP 
increases in 4Q26E, which could be the key share price catalyst. We believe that recent 
negatives news (including the impact of PTFE and CPO/NPO on the industry) are 
overdone. The impact on high-end CCL from these new technologies is likely to be limited 
from the point of view of Taiwan CCL companies. As such, we remain positive that the 
three names in our CCL coverage will ride on the decent price hike trend given favourable 
supply-demand dynamics.

• EMC (2383 TT, TWD5,700, Buy [1]) 
EMC’s September 2026 revenue was TWD21.5bn (+7% MoM, +169% YoY), which led to 
its 3Q26 sales (TWD60.9bn; +29% QoQ, +142% YoY) reaching 112% of our 3Q26 forecast 
(TWD54.4bn) and 110% of the Bloomberg consensus forecast (TWD55.5bn). We attribute 
the revenue strength to the higher-than-expected price hike and on-going spec upgrades 
(leading to higher ASPs). Management attributes c.50% of the MoM increase in revenue in 
September to the price hike and the other 50% came to product mix optimisation. Looking 
ahead, the company doesn’t rule out the potential to increase its ASP again in 4Q26 (after 
the first time in 2Q26) in order to reflect rising raw material prices since 2Q26. As such, we 
expect revenue to grow at least high teens again on the potential price head as well as 
ongoing spec upgrades.

EMC sees limited impact from PTFE and HC glass-fibre-free CCL. Based on our 
understanding, PTFE is still under certification accounting for only limited layers inside 
specified high-end PCB as of now. As such, its impact on the high-end CCL TAM should be 
limited. As for HC glass-fibre-free CCL, the rigidity of PCB using this type of CCL could be 
a key concern to us.

Lastly, regarding the recent Co-Packaged Optics/Near-Packaged Optics (CPO/NPO) 
news’s negative impact on the CCL TAM, we also believe it to be limited given the likely 
adoption date for CPO of 2030, based on EMC’s forecast. Besides, we believe the PCB 
board inside CPO scale-up switches at that time would still need to support an 800G/1.6T 
transmission speed (or even lower) which would still require high-end PCB transmission 
although the highest transmission bandwidth can increase to 3.2T via optics. Lastly, EMC 
believes the spec of CCL won’t downgrade to M4 in the worst-case scenario but would be 
at least M6. Last but not least, we believe this kind of CPO-connected AI server might only

Given its leading position in the CCL industry and the likely imbalance between supply and 
demand in 2027, we remain positive view on EMC. We have a Buy (1) rating on EMC, with 
a 12-month TP of TWD7,200, based on a target PER of 38x applied to our 1-year forward 
EPS forecast. Key downside risk: weaker-than-expected AI server demand.

• TUC (6274 TT, TWD1,780, Buy [1]) 
TUC’s September 2026 revenue was TWD6.1bn (+4% MoM, +115% YoY), which led to its 
3Q26 sales (TWD17.9bn; +26% QoQ, +123% YoY) reaching 103% of our 3Q26 forecast 
(TWD17.4bn) and 105% of the Bloomberg consensus forecast (TWD17.1bn). Looking 
ahead, we expect TUC to deliver at least QoQ growth in 4Q26 given 1) a price hike along 
with its peers for high-end CCL (eg. M8) and 2) some shipment delay from 3Q26 given 
flooding in Southeast Asia. For the longer term, the company is promising a continual spec 
upgrade trend in 2027 with new projects using M9-grade CCL (including low dk1, low dk2, 
and Q-glass version) in 2027 for high-end switches, AI servers, and optical transceivers. 
Considering the insufficient capacity in the CCL industry, we expect TUC to further optimise 
its product mix after acquiring more high-end CCL orders in future quarters (vs. a single 
major AI server client in 2026). We have a Buy (1) rating on TUC, with a 12-month TP of 
TWD2,320, based on a target PER of 35x applied to our 1-year forward EPS forecast. Key 
downside risks: 1) weaker-than-expected demand for switches/AI servers, and 2) market 
share loss to CCL peers.

• ITEQ (6213 TT, TWD751, Buy [1]) 
ITEQ’s September 2026 revenue was TWD5.8bn (+15% MoM, +111% YoY), which led to 
its 3Q26 sales (TWD15.6bn; +35% QoQ, +102% YoY) reaching 114% of our 3Q26 forecast 
(TWD13.7bn) and 108% of the Bloomberg consensus forecast (TWD14.5bn). The number 
for 3Q26 was higher than our estimate due likely to a stronger-than-expected price hike 
(c.30% in 3Q26 vs. expectation of 20%). Looking ahead to 4Q26, we expect the company 
to further raise its pricing by 20-30% to factor in cost inflation in raw materials (E-glass) 
and the supply-demand gap. The company also expects the rising supply of e-glass (which 
could lift its utilisation rate from the current level of 75%) to lift its gross margin more 
significantly. For 2027, the company is excited about the new CPU adoption driving rising 
M7 CCL usage and is working on M9 certification with new end customers, which could 
become a key growth driver in 2028. We have a Buy (1) rating on ITEQ. Key downside 
risk: weaker-than-expected regular server demand.

Source:                         Trendforce, Morgan Stanley Research
TV panel prices to stay  flat MoM in October: Mainstream 32", 43", 55", 65" and 75"
TV panel prices are expected to stay flat for the month of October. While panel
makers had previously notified their brands customers of potential price hikes from
4Q26 to mitigate elevated component costs, we believe that negotiations are
ongoing, as TV panel order momentum might slow down in the coming months on

seasonality and TV brands could reduce orders on higher panel prices. On the other
hand, panel makers continue to demonstrate good discipline, since Chinese panel
makers are committed to annual maintenance during the October holiday to control
output. We think it seems more likely that we will see TV panel price hikes toward
late 4Q26 when TV brands start to restock for CNY promotions. Our base-case
assumption is for the TV panel prices  to be flat QoQ in 4Q26, on a quarterly average

basis.
IT panel prices to remain flat MoM in October: Monitor and notebook panel prices
are expected to be flat MoM during the month. Both applications continue to  see
slower demand in  2H26 post the earlier restocking  in 1H26 amid concerns about
increasing component costs. That said, panel makers are under cost pressure and
reluctant to given more price concessions. On a quarterly average basis, we expect
IT panel prices to remain flat QoQ in 4Q26.

Risk/reward  less attractive: We maintain the view that the current valuation of
Taiwanese panel makers, AUO (2409.TW) at 2.0x P/B and Innolux (3481.TW) at 1.7x
P/B, have already baked in the expectations of glass in advanced packaging for
revenue contribution in 2028. However, some supply-chain players think this might
take longer, and there seem to be more suppliers from different supply chains vying
for this opportunity. In China, while expectations are also building for advanced

packaging, we have a    relative preference for BOE (000725.SZ; 1.4x P/B) over TCL
(000100.SZ; 1.7x P/B), on its scale  and faster progress in glass core substrates.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of
interest that could affect the objectivity of Morgan Stanley
Research. Investors should consider Morgan Stanley
Research as only a single factor in making their investment

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
Key Takeaways
We believe current valuations of 1.5-2.0x P/B have already priced in expectations
of glass in advanced packaging from 2H28 for the panel makers.
Nonetheless,  the risk in commercialization and competition remain key to watch.

Morgan Stanley Research 3
TV Panel Prices Trended Down in 3Q26
TV panel prices started to edge up at the beginning of this year, rising 1% MoM in January,
2% MoM in February and 2% MoM in March. April, May and June were largely flat, while
July was down 2% MoM, August was down 1% MoM, and September was flat MoM,   in line
with our expectation that TV panel prices would start trending down from 3Q26.  That
said, we do not expect a significant decline as supply-side discipline should provide

downside support.

Below-seasonal Panel Shipments in 2H26
Over the past decade, TV panel shipments have typically been split 49%:51% between 1H
and 2H. In 2026, we expect the mix to skew toward 1H, as TV brands restocked ahead of
major promotional events, including the Winter Olympics in February, the FIFA World Cup
in June-July, and China's 618 shopping festival. We see no clear evidence that these events

will materially boost global TV demand, but they have historically shifted shipment timing
across quarters. As a result, we expect the restocking momentum seen since the start of
the year to fade, with panel order momentum slowing in 2H26.
Display Fab Utilization  ~85% in 3Q26
Stronger TV panel shipments supported display fab utilization of ~80% in 4Q24 and 82%
in 1Q25. Utilization declined to 78% in 2Q25 as brands adopted a more cautious stance
following earlier pull-ins and as the impact of China's trade-in program moderated.

Utilization rebounded to 80-85% in 3Q25 as brands restocked for year-end promotions
and remained at similar levels in 4Q25 despite seasonal softness as well as in 1Q26.
Industry utilization stayed broadly flat QoQ, at 80-85% in 2Q26, reflecting continued
supply discipline, followed by a slight uptick in 3Q26.
Over the medium to long term, we continue to believe the display industry is undergoing
structural change. Chinese panel makers have shifted their focus from top-line growth to

profitability, while lower government subsidies have reinforced supply discipline. We
believe investors increasingly recognize that the industry's production-to-order model can
remain sustainable, particularly as panel price cycles shortened to quarters in 2023-24 vs.
multiyear cycles in the previous decade. Consolidation actions, including LG Display's sale
of its Gen 8.5 Guangzhou fab to TCL and Sharp's closure of its Gen 10 Sakai fab, also

support a healthier supply backdrop (for more information, see Display – Structural
Changes on Supply Side Not Fairly Priced In, June 17, 2021).

### Page 5

M
Idea
Morgan Stanley Research 5
Risk/Reward Largely Fair
We view risk-reward as broadly balanced following the recent correction in panel stocks.
Below are our current views:
• AUO (2409.TW, Derrick Yang): We rate AUO Equal-weight with a PT of NT
$27.00, based on a 1.4x 2026e P/B multiple. While we believe the current round of
TV panel price increases is nearing an end, major panel makers should continue to
demonstrate solid supply discipline, providing downside support. We appreciate

AUO's efforts to diversify its business, but meaningful earnings contributions are
likely to take time.
• BOE (000725.SZ, Derrick Yang): We prefer BOE due to its scale advantage,
higher exposure to display and faster progress in glass core substrates. Our PT of
Rmb9.30 is based on 2.5x 2026e P/B, which we view as reasonable given our
estimated ROE of 5-8% over 2026-28 compared with BOE's average P/B of 1.1x and
ROE of 2-4% during 2023-25.
• Innolux (3481.TW, Derrick Yang): In semiconductors, our supply-chain checks

suggest that Innolux is participating in a foundry customer's glass core substrate
project. However, we do not expect meaningful contributions before 2028, and
additional competitors could enter as the industry moves toward mass production.
We maintain our Equal-weight rating and PT of NT$60.00, based on a 2.1x 2026e
P/B multiple.
• LG Display (034220.KS, Ryan Kim): Meaningful OLED revenue contribution
should increasingly improve earnings from 2H25, but potentially weaker end-

markets keep us from turning bullish on LGD. The stock trades at 0.6x 2026e P/B
vs. a historical mid-cycle level of 0.5x.
• TCL (000100.SZ, Derrick Yang): We are EW with a price target of Rmb4.70 (1.7x
2026e P/B). The stock is trading at 1.7x 2026e P/B vs. its mid-cycle average of 1.4x
since 2022.
• Greater China DDIC supply chain implications – Novatek (3034.TW, Daniel Yen):
Our Underweight thesis reflects weaker PC, smartphone and automotive demand,

gross margin pressure, and a lack of new growth drivers. That said, Novatek's
OLED DDI roadmap appears more competitive than those of its key Korean peers
in terms of both performance and cost, helping it gain share in Apple's iPhone
supply chain.

### Page 7

M
Idea
Morgan Stanley Research 7
Where We Could Be Wrong
• Stronger end demand: If end demand for major applications turns out to be better
than expected, there could be stronger panel shipments and less panel price
pressure.
• More stringent output control: If major panel makers collectively adjust
utilization to an extreme extent, they could mitigate pricing pressure in the case of
softer demand.
• Non-commodity display business: If the non-commodity business progresses

faster than expected, panel makers could show better financial results.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over
the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated

below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -

relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Chroma ATE (2360.TW)

3
Chroma ATE
Valuation
Our target price of NT$2,500 is based on 40x 2027E P/E, at close to 1x PEG. Chroma shares have re-rated above its historical
range as its growth outlook is now strongly tied to AI infrastructure build out. We estimate 60%+ of its revenue is now AI-
related, with strong growth across product segments including power testers, SLT, metrology, and photonics. Chroma has a
commanding market share in most of these segments and should be well positioned to capitalize on the global AI expansion.

Risks
Downside risks to achieving our target price include: 1) softer AI/CPO growth expectation due to changes in capex outlook or
production bottleneck; 2) fewer SLT needed as a result of shortened cycle time as GPU yield rate improves; 3) disappointing
metrology shipment due to changes at ramp-up schedule; 4) testing process change leading to loss in market share; 5) FX risks
(50-60% of Chroma’s sales are in US$, 10-20% in Rmb).
Prepared for Kevin Lu

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

## Document 078: 261006_daiwa_kinsus

Source PDF: all report pdf/261006_daiwa_kinsus.pdf
Report source: 未公開來源
Date: 2026-10-06
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We believe an improving product mix, driven by rising 
Nvidia’s CPU contribution, to be a key near-term share price catalyst.

What’s the impact: Near-term update. After Kinsus released its August 
preliminary results on 23 September, we note that we had underestimated 
the 3Q26E gross margin expansion on product optimisation and price hike 
scale. The results imply a GM of 33% in August (vs. 26% in 2Q26). We 
therefore lift our 3Q-4Q26E gross margin to 33.2-35.1% (from 28.9-30.3%) 
to reflect an improving product mix, including greater CPU substrate 
contribution. We believe the gross margin of high-end chipset substrate 
may be c.50%, supporting margin expansion. We also expect a c.10% QoQ 
price hike for both BT and ABF substrate in 3Q26, above Kinsus’ guidance 
of a high-single-digit% QoQ, making these substrates the other growth 
driver. We see further gross margin expansion in 2027-28 on the ongoing 
price hike trend and increasing high-end ABF substrate product mix.

Business outlook. We believe several favourable trends lie ahead. First, 
we see a rising contribution from high-end GPU and CPU ABF substrates. 
We believe Nvidia’s CPU currently accounts for 5-10% of total revenue, 
with Kinsus holding a 70-80% market share. We also expect Kinsus to 
acquire a teens% market share in AMD’s Venice, which should gradually 
ramp up from 4Q26. These will likely become key growth drivers of the ABF 
substrate segment in 2027, in our view. We believe the ramp-up progress 
for GPU substrate for these two clients is slightly slower than that for CPU. 
Second, we believe Kinsus will benefit from the rising BT substrate demand 
for optical transceivers. In our view, migration to on-board optics (OBO) and 
NPO/CPO will require ABF substrate beneath the optics engine (OE) in 
OBO; the OE and IC will need a bigger ABF substrate as we move to the 
NPO/CPO generation. As such, we expect the total ABF substrate demand 
to increase with rising adoption of optics in AI servers. Lastly, we expect the 
adoption of multiple-core ABF from 2028 to improve the rigidity and 
transmission performance before the official launch of ceramic and glass-
core substrate, which we expect to increase the ABF TAM as well.

What we recommend: We raise our 2026-28E earnings by 19-29% to 
reflect the better-than-expected gross margin in August. We reaffirm our 
Buy (1) call and raise our 12M TP to TWD1,580, based on an unchanged 
target PER of 36x (high end of 3-year-trading range of 10-36x), applied to 
the 1-year-forward EPS forecast. Key downside risks: weaker-than-
expected demand for memory, PCs, gaming GPUs or AI servers.

How we differ: Our 2026-28E EPS are 27-57% above the Bloomberg 
consensus, likely on our more bullish view on its gross margin expansion.

6 October 2026

3Q26 results preview: several favourable trends ahead

➢ Rising high-end ABF substrate likely to drive blended ASP increase   
➢ Multicore and optics likely to be the next growth drivers 
➢ Reaffirming our Buy (1) call; raising 12M TP to TWD1,580

Company profile  
Kinsus was founded in 2000 as a subsidiary of Asustek. The company mainly focus on niche ball grid 
array (BGA) substrates to avoid price competition among mainstream products like graphics card and 
chipsets, and it has become top-3 flip chip manufacturer in global. Besides, its manufacturing sites are 
based in Taiwan and mainland China.

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
Kinsus's leadership team comprises experienced professionals dedicated to steering the
company towards sustainable growth. This diverse board composition ensures a comprehensive
range of expertise and perspectives, contributing to Kinsus’s strategic decision-making and
corporate governamce.
Capital management 2
Kinsus maintains a robust financial framework, ensuring the availability of resources necessary
for operational efficiency and strategic investments. The company's financial health is regularly
disclosed through annual reports, providing transparency to stakeholders.
Related party &
transaction 1
The company adheres to stringent corporate governance policies to manage related party
transactions, ensuring transparency and compliance with regulatory standards. The company
discloses such transactions in its financial reports, maintaining accountability and stakeholder
trust. For instance, the company's annual general meeting documents provide details on related
party transactions, ensuring transparency and accountability.
S Supply chainmanagement 1
Kinsus is committed to sustainable supply chain practices, emphasizing environmental, social,
and governance considerations. The company has established comprehensive ESG policies,
including commitments to labor standards, environmental health and safety, ethics, and conflict-
free minerals. These policies guide the company's interactions with suppliers and partners,
promoting sustainability throughout the supply chain.
E Data security 3
The company recognizes the importance of information security in its operations. As part of its
corporate governance framework, Kinsus is likely to implement standard data protection
practices to safeguard sensitive information and ensure compliance with relevant regulations.
Stakeholders are encouraged to consult the company's official communications or contact the
company directly for detailed information on data security policies.

M
Update
Bizlink  | Asia Pacific
September Sales +5% MoM/
+39% YoY
In this report, we focus on Bizlink's monthly sales, which we
believe could be a catalyst for its share price.
Details:
• September sales came in at NT$9,332mn (+5% MoM/+39% YoY)
• 3Q26 revenue reached NT$27,854mn (+20% QoQ/+50% YoY), slightly short
of MSe of NT$28,521mn (+23% QoQ/+55% YoY) by 2% while exceeding
consensus of NT$27,423mn (+18% QoQ/+49% YoY) by 2%.
Our view:
• We think 3Q26 revenue came in largely in-line with market expectations.

• Looking into 2H26, we expect HPC to remain the main growth driver, as
power interconnects should continue to see momentum from AI/general
servers. Meanwhile, data interconnects should grow along with new ASIC
model ramp-up and GPU server demand. Moreover, semi cap equipment
revenue growth should also sustain.
• We believe Bizlink has allocated increasing resources toward the optical
interconnect space, including the collaboration on ALC with Credo, mini

VCSEL optical module with ams-OSRAM and shuffle box with Corning. We
think that these opportunities could help offset a potential slowdown in its
AEC business as AEC solutions reach their scalability limits at higher data
rates, likely from  3.2T.
• Stay OW.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Bizlink (3665.TW)
Base case, derived from 30x 2027e P/E. We think our target P/E multiple is justified by an
earnings CAGR of 46% seen over 2025-28e, which implies a PEG of around 0.7. This is in line
with the average of 0.7 in 2019-24, when the stock was trading at an average 13x P/E for a 19%
earnings CAGR.

n Faster and wider AEC adoption
n Data center and semi equipment capex increases that drive stronger HPC and semi-cap

business
n Stronger recovery for automotive business
Risks to Downside
n Lower-than-expected AEC adoption
n Intensifying competition
n Softer-than-expected AI capex
n Slower-than-expected growth in the semi capital equipment market
n Continued weakness in the auto industry

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

below.

Increasing power consumption, along with the deployment of
BBU/CBU/800/±400VDC/SST  in AI clusters, could push the
technology barrier higher and present growth opportunities for
players with superior capabilities. Fuel cells could also emerge

as a new opportunity amid power constraints.
Secular demand for power amid AI capex: AI-related power consumption will
continue to increase, and we believe this will lead to secular demand for power-
related components, such as power supply units (PSUs), battery backup units
(BBUs), capacitor bank units (CBUs), coolant distribution units (CDUs), and so on.
Moreover, the requirement for efficiency will inevitably necessitate the adoption of
800/±400 voltage direct current (VDC)  in the power rack/center/block

configuration (initially from late 2026 and broadly in 2028, we estimate), eventually
extending to the deployment of  solid state transformers (SST) in the longer term –
all pointing to a more centralized power delivery architecture in the future. This
trend of aggregation could raise the technology barriers amid the fusion of white/
grey   space, favoring players with better capabilities and execution. Overall, we
forecast the datacenter PSU market size to grow at a 75% three-year CAGR to reach

US$33bn in 2028.
Fuel cells to improve flexibility and resilience of AI power delivery: Despite the
strong AI capex outlook (MSe global cloud capex +97%/20% YoY in 2026/2027),
power has become a bottleneck, indicated by MS's forecast power shortfall of 5GW
in 2026, scaling to a total 72GW through 2029 (see report). Fuel cells, among other
alternative energy sources, could emerge as a good solution to pick up some slack
and enhance efficiency through active micro grid management. Those who are able

to offer comprehensive solutions from power sources to power delivery could be
increasingly more competitive in the market.
Assuming coverage of Delta and Lite-On:
• Delta (OW, PT NT$2,500, based on 30x 2027/2028 P/E) should maintain its
dominant position in the industry with its integrated power/thermal related
solutions while fuel cells could add incremental growth opportunities in the
longer term. We think a 30x P/E target multiple is justified by our estimated

63% earnings CAGR in 2025-28, driven by a 44% revenue CAGR and
operating margin expansion to 22.1% in 2028 vs 15.1% in 2025,
• Lite-On (EW, PT NT$315, based on 17x 2027/2028 P/E), the second-largest
player in AI power-related products, should also ride the tailwinds despite its
slower progress  in HVDC power racks and liquid cooling solutions. We think
a 17x P/E  target multiple is justified by our estimated 47% earnings CAGR in
2025-28 (29% revenue CAGR and 6.8ppt OPM expansion  to 16.8% in 2028).

Asia Pacific Insight
Morgan Stanley Research 3
Executive Summary
AI cluster performance evolving to fulfill  demand
The rapid adoption of artificial intelligence is driving a significant increase in global data
center compute demand. Advances in foundation models, reasoning capabilities, agentic
AI, and enterprise AI deployment are increasing the computational resources required for
both training and inference. To support this growth, hyperscalers and model developers

continue to deploy larger and more powerful AI clusters, driving demand for greater
compute, memory, and networking capacity.
As AI infrastructure scales, cluster performance is increasingly determined by the
efficiency of the entire system rather than the capabilities of individual processors alone.
Compute, networking, cooling, and power infrastructure must all evolve together to
support higher rack densities and larger deployments. While semiconductor innovation

remains critical, supporting infrastructure is becoming an equally important factor in
enabling future AI growth.
AI cluster performance is fundamentally a power problem
As AI clusters continue to scale in size and performance, power consumption is becoming
one of the most important constraints on future infrastructure deployment. The power
challenge is no longer limited to GPUs alone, as modern AI clusters comprise tightly
integrated compute, networking, memory, storage, and cooling systems, all of which

require increasing amounts of power as performance requirements rise. Consequently, the
next phase of AI infrastructure investment should be viewed not only as a semiconductor
upgrade cycle but also as a broad-based power infrastructure upgrade cycle.
Server power consumption has increased significantly across successive AI hardware
generations. Nvidia's architecture roadmap illustrates the industry's migration from
relatively modest server-level power budgets under the Ampere and Hopper generations

toward rack-scale systems under Blackwell and future Rubin architectures. Estimated rack-
level power consumption has increased from roughly 40kW for Hopper systems to
approximately 120kW for GB200 NVL72 and 140kW for GB300 NVL72, with future Vera
Rubin systems expected to approach 200-300kW per rack with current Vera Rubin
systems reaching up to 330kW per rack while future Vera Rubin generations are expected
to surpass these levels. As compute density continues to increase, power delivery

infrastructure is becoming a critical enabler of system performance.
The increase in power consumption is not limited to compute hardware. Networking
systems are also becoming increasingly power intensive as switch bandwidth scales from
100G architectures toward 800G and 1.6T generations. Despite substantial process-node
improvements, switch ASIC power consumption has continued to rise due to increasing
complexity and higher SerDes speeds. As a result, networking equipment now requires

significantly larger power budgets than previous generations, creating additional demand
for higher-capacity power supplies throughout the data center.

### Page 4

M
Asia Pacific Insight
4

Switch system bandwidth (Tbps)

Source: Morgan Stanley Research

The growth in power consumption is driving a corresponding upgrade cycle for power-
supply units (PSUs). Earlier AI server generations typically relied on multiple 3-3.3KW
power supplies installed directly within individual server chassis. As the industry
transitions toward rack-scale architectures, power conversion is increasingly centralized
into dedicated power shelves capable of supporting substantially larger power loads. In
Blackwell systems, power shelves utilize 5.5KW power supplies, while future Rubin-

generation platforms are expected to increase PSU ratings to approximately 18KW,
representing a significant increase in both PSU content and power density.
Delivering higher power within a similar footprint creates significant engineering
challenges. Increasing PSU power density requires advances across multiple areas,
including higher-performance power semiconductors such as silicon carbide (SiC) and
gallium nitride (GaN), more efficient power-conversion topologies, smaller and more

capable magnetic components, improved thermal-management solutions, and greater
system integration. As output power increases, thermal constraints become increasingly
important, making heat dissipation, airflow optimization, and mechanical design critical
determinants of PSU performance.
Consequently, on top of the volume driven growth, the power-supply industry is
benefiting from the technology driven dynamics, where efficiency, power density, and

thermal performance are becoming key competitive differentiators. As AI rack power
requirements continue to increase, PSU innovation will become an increasingly important
component of the broader AI infrastructure investment cycle.

CAGR of 75% to reach US$33bn in 2028 from US$6bn in 2025, driven mainly by server
shipment growth, increasing power consumption per rack, and the adoption of HVDC

power racks from 2027.

Complementary hardware for more resilient power delivery
As AI clusters become more power intensive, maintaining stable and resilient power
delivery is emerging as a critical design priority. Traditional centralized power

architectures are increasingly being complemented by localized energy-storage and
power-conditioning technologies that can respond rapidly to dynamic workload demands.
Battery Backup Units (BBUs) represent one of the most important additions to modern AI
power architectures. Positioned close to the computing workload, BBUs provide localized
energy storage capable of responding significantly faster than facility-level backup
systems. Their role extends beyond conventional backup power functionality. BBUs can

### Page 6

M
Asia Pacific Insight
6
absorb transient fluctuations, smooth power ramps, and improve overall rack-level power
stability. By providing local buffering capacity, BBUs help reduce the burden on upstream
power distribution infrastructure while improving overall system resilience.
Exhibit 11: Concept of peak shaving for AI server power loads
Source: Nvidia
Exhibit 12: BBU
Source: Panasonic
Capacitor Backup Units (CBUs) address an even shorter-duration segment of the power

stability challenge. Utilizing supercapacitors and other high-power-density storage
technologies, CBUs can deliver and absorb power within milliseconds, making them
particularly effective for managing rapid GPU power spikes. These systems act as shock
absorbers within the electrical architecture, preventing sudden load changes from
propagating throughout the broader power network. As AI rack power densities continue
increasing, capacitor-based energy storage is becoming an attractive solution for

improving power quality without requiring substantial oversizing of facility infrastructure.
Exhibit 13: CBU
Source: Panasonic
Exhibit 14: GB300 power shelf with integrated capacitors
Source: Nvidia
Together, BBUs and CBUs provide complementary benefits. BBUs are designed to handle
longer-duration events, while CBUs are optimized for extremely fast transient response.
The integration of these technologies enables operators to improve power quality, reduce

stranded infrastructure capacity, enhance system reliability, and increase effective
utilization of expensive electrical assets. As rack densities continue to rise, localized
energy-storage solutions are likely to become a standard component of AI data center
design rather than an optional enhancement.

### Page 7

M
Asia Pacific Insight
Morgan Stanley Research 7
The adoption of BBU and CBU architectures may also enable future increases in rack
density. As operators attempt to push toward several hundred kilowatts per rack,
localized power-buffering solutions can help mitigate transient peaks and reduce stress on
grid-facing infrastructure. This allows operators to deploy higher-density computing
resources without proportionately increasing upstream electrical capacity.

HVDC emerging as a necessity, with SST in the pipeline
800VDC and ±400VDC progressing simultaneously
The rapid increase in AI rack power density is creating growing challenges for conventional
power-distribution architectures. As rack-level power consumption rises from tens of
kilowatts to several hundreds of KWs, electrical losses, cable size, thermal management
requirements, and overall system complexity increase significantly. Under traditional low-

voltage power architectures, delivering larger amounts of power requires proportionally
higher current, which increases resistive losses and places greater stress on cables,
connectors, busbars, and power-conversion equipment.
At the same time, every power-conversion stage introduces efficiency losses and
generates additional heat. As AI deployments scale into multi-megawatt and eventually
gigawatt-level campuses, even small efficiency improvements can translate into

meaningful reductions in operating costs, cooling requirements, and infrastructure
investment. Consequently, the industry is increasingly focused on power architectures
that can reduce conversion stages, lower current levels, and improve overall power-
delivery efficiency.
Against this backdrop, high-voltage direct current (HVDC) is emerging as one of the most
important architectural upgrades for next-generation AI infrastructure. By increasing
distribution voltage, HVDC allows the same amount of power to be delivered at a lower

current, reducing conduction losses and improving system efficiency. Higher-voltage
architectures also improve scalability for ultra-high-density rack deployments by
alleviating some of the physical and thermal constraints associated with conventional
power-distribution systems.

### Page 8

M
Asia Pacific Insight
8
Exhibit 16: Better power efficiency for 800VDC architecture
Source: Delta
Exhibit 17: Better efficiency leads to cost savings
Source: Delta
Nvidia's roadmap illustrates the industry's gradual migration toward higher-voltage power
architectures. Earlier AI server generations primarily utilized conventional power-delivery
schemes centered around rack-level 48V distribution. As rack power requirements
increased with Blackwell-generation systems, industry participants began evaluating

higher-voltage architectures capable of supporting substantially greater power density.
Looking ahead, the industry is increasingly converging around 800VDC as a potential
next-generation standard for large-scale AI deployments, particularly as rack-level power
consumption approaches several hundred kilowatts. The transition reflects the need to
improve power-distribution efficiency while supporting the continued growth of AI
computing infrastructure.

We believe that the adoption of the 800VDC power rack could start from late 4Q26,
though it should be small scope deployment through 2027, since the in-rack power shelf
is the reference design in the Vera Rubin NVL72 rack. Broader adoption should happen
more likely from late 2027 and 2028, as the Vera Rubin Ultra rack further pushes up the
power consumption.
Exhibit 18: HVDC deployment roadmap
Source: Nvidia

### Page 9

M
Asia Pacific Insight
Morgan Stanley Research 9
The 800VDC power rack BOM is primarily driven by the PSU, which accounts for 50–55%
of total cost, followed by the BBU at 25–30%, with other components comprising the
remaining 15–20%. This cost structure reflects the broader functionality integrated into an
HVDC power rack, combining power conversion, backup power and supporting
components within a centralized architecture.
For power supply players, one of the most direct implications is that an HVDC power rack

carries around 4–5x higher dollar content per watt compared with a conventional in-rack
PSU solution, since the former integrates more components into a robust system and
power supply players usually ship the entire rack instead of just the components.

Source: Morgan Stanley Research
However, the industry has not yet fully converged on a single architecture. While Nvidia's
roadmap increasingly points toward 800VDC, several hyperscalers and members of the
Open Compute Project (OCP) ecosystem have expressed interest in ±400VDC
architectures. Both approaches deliver similar total voltage levels and pursue many of the

same objectives, including lower current, higher efficiency, and improved scalability. The
debate largely centers on implementation trade-offs rather than end goals.
Supporters of 800VDC generally highlight the advantages of a simpler single-bus
architecture, potentially lower system complexity, and closer alignment with existing
industrial high-voltage DC equipment. The architecture avoids the need to maintain
balanced positive and negative voltage rails and may simplify certain aspects of power

conversion and system integration. These characteristics make 800VDC particularly
attractive in environments where operational simplicity and interoperability with existing
electrical infrastructure are key priorities.
Advocates of ±400VDC, meanwhile, emphasize the advantages of a bipolar architecture.
By creating positive and negative voltage rails around a neutral reference point, ±400VDC
can potentially reduce insulation requirements, simplify certain fault-management

scenarios, and provide improved flexibility for load balancing and future system expansion.
Many hyperscalers also view the bipolar approach as being more compatible with long-
term visions for highly distributed power systems incorporating energy storage, renewable
energy, and microgrid functionality.
Importantly, the ultimate beneficiaries may be largely independent of which standard
gains wider adoption. With the exception of certain power-semiconductor and protection-

system requirements, many component suppliers across the power ecosystem remain

### Page 10

M
Asia Pacific Insight
10
broadly agnostic between 800VDC and ±400VDC implementations. Higher-voltage
architectures in either form require similar upgrades in power conversion, power
management, cooling, connectors, magnetics, and infrastructure equipment. As a result,
the industry's migration toward HVDC matters significantly more than the eventual
selection of any single voltage standard.

Solid-state transformers to follow after 800VDC/±400VDC matures
Looking further ahead, solid-state transformers (SSTs) represent a potential next-
generation evolution of power infrastructure. Unlike traditional transformers that operate

primarily as passive devices, SSTs leverage power electronics to provide voltage
conversion, power conditioning, and bidirectional energy management within a single
platform. This enables greater controllability and flexibility while supporting increasingly
digitalized power networks.
Although SSTs remain at an early stage of commercialization and face challenges related
to economics, reliability, and large-scale deployment, the technology aligns well with long-

term data center trends. As AI campuses evolve into utility-scale power consumers
incorporating energy storage, distributed generation, and advanced power management,
SSTs could eventually become a key enabling technology.
Therefore, while HVDC is expected to drive the next phase of power architecture
upgrades, SSTs represent a longer-term technology roadmap that could further transform
how future data centers distribute and manage electricity.

Fuel cells for better flexibility and resilience
Fuel cells generate electricity through an electrochemical reaction rather than
combustion. Hydrogen, natural gas, biogas, or other fuels are supplied to the fuel cell
stack, where chemical energy is converted directly into electricity, heat, and in some cases
water. Unlike batteries, which store energy, fuel cells continuously generate power as long
as fuel is supplied.
Exhibit 25: Fuel cell diagram

Source: 3M
Fuel cells can offer several benefits, including:
• High energy efficiency: Direct electrochemical conversion enables higher
efficiency than conventional combustion-based power generation, reducing fuel
consumption and operating costs.
• Low carbon emissions: Fuel cells produce minimal direct emissions and can
support long-term decarbonization goals when powered by low-carbon fuels such
as hydrogen.
• Grid independence and energy security: Provides reliable onsite power

generation, reducing dependence on grid availability and improving energy
resilience.
• Long-duration energy storage enabler: Hydrogen-based fuel-cell systems can
complement renewable energy by storing energy over extended periods,
supporting long-duration and seasonal energy storage.
• Fuel flexibility: Certain fuel-cell technologies can operate on hydrogen, natural
gas, biogas, ammonia-derived hydrogen, and other hydrogen-rich fuels, allowing
deployment using existing fuel infrastructure.

• Low maintenance and quiet operation: Limited moving parts reduce maintenance
requirements while providing quieter operation than conventional combustion-
based generators.
• Scalable deployment: Can be deployed across a wide range of power levels, from
distributed backup systems to utility-scale installations, making fuel cells suitable
for large AI data center campuses

### Page 13

M
Asia Pacific Insight
Morgan Stanley Research 13
However, the following challenging are hurdles for wider deployment:
• High system cost: Fuel cells remain more expensive than conventional power-
generation technologies, limiting large-scale adoption.
• Limited hydrogen infrastructure: Hydrogen production, transportation, storage,
and refueling networks remain underdeveloped in most regions.
• Durability requirements: Long-term reliability and stack lifetime remain key

challenges, particularly for large-scale and mission-critical deployments.
• Regulatory and safety considerations: Hydrogen handling requires specialized
infrastructure and compliance with stringent safety regulations, increasing
deployment complexity.
• Lack of standardization: Multiple fuel-cell technologies compete across different
applications, creating uncertainty around long-term technology standards and
adoption paths.
Fuel cells have the potential to fundamentally reshape future data center power

architectures. Traditionally, data centers rely on grid electricity as the primary power
source and diesel generators for backup power. However, as AI-driven workloads
significantly increase power demand and power availability becomes a key deployment
constraint, hyperscalers are increasingly evaluating fuel cells not only as a backup-power
solution but also as a potential primary power source. This could accelerate the transition
from a grid-centric architecture toward one incorporating distributed onsite generation,

enabling data center deployment in regions where utility power capacity is limited.
Over the longer term, fuel cells could become a core component of AI data center
microgrids that integrate renewable generation, battery storage, hydrogen storage, and
intelligent energy-management systems. Such an architecture would improve scalability,
resilience, and energy independence while reducing reliance on centralized utility
infrastructure. In addition, because fuel cells inherently generate DC power, they align

well with the industry's migration toward HVDC-based power distribution architectures.
This could simplify power delivery by reducing conversion stages between generation and
IT equipment, improving overall system efficiency and supporting next-generation high-
density AI deployment
Exhibit 26: Fuel cell in datacenter power delivery architecture
Source: Delta

Morgan Stanley Research 17
AI Cluster Performance Evolving to Fulfill Demand
AI adoption driving exponential growth in compute demand
AI is becoming a mainstream computing workload
Over the past three years, generative AI has evolved from a research application into a
core technology platform. Consumer applications such as ChatGPT, Claude, Gemini, and
Copilot now serve hundreds of millions of users globally, while enterprises are

increasingly embedding AI into software development, customer service, document
analysis, enterprise search, healthcare, financial research, and industrial automation.
Historically, software demand scaled roughly with the number of users. AI demand is
fundamentally different because every user interaction consumes computational
resources in real time. Unlike traditional cloud applications, where the bulk of
computation occurs during software development, AI applications continuously consume

compute whenever a model generates a response. Consequently, rising AI adoption
directly translates into higher demand for data center compute resources.
Model complexity continues to increase
The AI industry has historically improved model performance through scaling laws: larger
models trained on larger datasets using more compute generally produce better
outcomes. While efficiency improvements continue, leading AI developers remain
committed to scaling model capabilities.

More importantly, the industry is moving beyond conventional language models toward
significantly more compute-intensive architectures.
Reasoning models
Traditional LLMs generate answers directly from a prompt. New reasoning models
perform multiple internal computational steps before producing a response. Instead of
generating a few hundred output tokens, advanced reasoning systems may generate
thousands or even tens of thousands of internal reasoning tokens while evaluating

different solution paths. This dramatically increases inference workloads.
The industry is increasingly shifting from "parameter scaling" toward "test-time compute
scaling," where additional compute is consumed during inference to improve answer
quality. From an infrastructure perspective, this is highly significant because inference
workloads occur continuously rather than once during model training.

### Page 18

M
Asia Pacific Insight
18
Exhibit 28: Increasing demand for compute power from reasoning models
1x
2-5x
5-20x
10-50x
Basic chatbot response Coding assistant Reasonaing model Multiagent work flow
Relative Compute Requirement
Source: Morgan Stanley Research estimates
Agentic AI
The next wave of AI applications involves autonomous agents capable of planning,
executing, verifying, and refining tasks.
Instead of generating a single answer, an AI agent may search multiple information

sources, run code, validate results, generate follow-up tasks, and produce a final response.
Each step requires additional inference cycles.
A workflow that previously required one model invocation may now require dozens of
model calls, substantially increasing compute consumption per user.
Multimodal AI
AI models are increasingly processing images, video, audio, sensor data, and text
simultaneously.
Video understanding is particularly compute intensive because each second of video

contains vastly more information than a text prompt.
As multimodal AI adoption grows, compute demand per query rises significantly, creating
another structural driver of accelerator demand.
Training demand remains robust
Although inference is becoming the largest source of AI compute demand, training
workloads continue to expand. Frontier model development increasingly requires clusters
containing tens of thousands of accelerators operating simultaneously.

Training runs are becoming larger because developers are pursuing larger parameter
counts, more training data, longer training durations, multimodal datasets and
reinforcement learning and reasoning optimization
Consequently, each new generation of foundation models typically consumes
substantially more compute than the previous generation.

### Page 19

M
Asia Pacific Insight
Morgan Stanley Research 19
Importantly, training infrastructure sets the baseline for future inference deployment.
Every major model developer must maintain access to large-scale training clusters to
remain competitive, supporting continued demand for high-end AI accelerators.
More compute requests to be fulfilled by better chip
performance
Rising compute demand cannot be addressed simply by adding more servers. Modern AI
workloads increasingly encounter bottlenecks related to memory bandwidth, interconnect

performance, power consumption, and cooling. As a result, each new generation of AI
hardware delivers improvements across multiple dimensions.
Compute performance
The latest AI accelerators provide several-fold performance improvements versus prior
generations. For example, NVIDIA's Blackwell architecture was specifically designed to
support both large-scale training and high-volume inference, with significant gains in AI
throughput compared with previous Hopper-based systems. Performance improvements

are necessary because reasoning and agentic workloads require substantially more
operations per query.
Memory capacity and bandwidth
AI models are increasingly constrained by memory rather than raw compute, because
longer models, longer context windows, reasoning models and agentic workflows all
require more memory. As model complexity increases, modern high-end accelerators
usually incorporate hundreds of gigabytes of High Bandwidth Memory (HBM), providing

memory bandwidth measured in multiple terabytes per second.
Interconnect performance
As AI clusters scale from hundreds to thousands of accelerators, communication between
processors becomes increasingly important. A large AI training cluster effectively behaves
like a single massive computer. Consequently, networking technologies such as NVLink,
InfiniBand, Ethernet, etc., have become critical enablers of AI scaling.
Many AI workloads can no longer be accelerated simply by increasing GPU performance;

cluster interconnect bandwidth must also improve.

AI Cluster Performance Is Fundamentally a Power Problem
As AI clusters become larger and more powerful, every component within the cluster
must deliver higher throughput, lower latency, and greater bandwidth. Achieving these
improvements inevitably requires more electrical power.
Therefore, the power challenge is no longer limited to GPUs. Modern AI clusters are highly
integrated systems comprising compute servers, networking fabrics, memory subsystems,

storage infrastructure, and cooling equipment. As cluster performance increases, power
consumption rises across the entire infrastructure stack.
In our view, the next phase of AI infrastructure investment should be viewed not simply as
a semiconductor upgrade cycle, but as a broad-based power consumption cycle driven by
the need for ever-higher AI cluster performance.
Server systems seeing increasing TDP
Nvidia’s AI-server power roadmap is best understood as a transition from chip-defined, air-

cooled nodes to power-, cooling- and fabric-constrained rack-scale computers: the
Ampere-era A100 SXM carried a disclosed 400W standard TDP—up to 500W in custom
thermal designs—with 40/80GB HBM2/HBM2e and 600GB/s NVLink, while the eight-
GPU, dual-AMD-EPYC DGX A100 was a conventional 6U server with a 6.5KW maximum
input rating, meaning GPU silicon represented only roughly half of the node envelope
once CPUs, NVSwitches, memory, NICs, drives, fans and conversion losses were included.

Hopper stepped the H100 SXM to a disclosed 700W TDP, 80GB HBM3, roughly 3TB/s
memory bandwidth and 900GB/s fourth-generation NVLink, while H200 retained
essentially the same Hopper compute architecture and power profile but substituted
141GB of faster HBM3e at 4.8TB/s—making H200 primarily a memory-capacity/bandwidth
upgrade rather than a compute-TDP step-up—and Nvidia rates the eight-GPU, dual-Xeon
DGX H100/H200 at 10.2KW maximum input, versus 6.5KW for DGX A100;

The architectural discontinuity arrives with GB200. Rather than treating an eight-GPU
server as the scale-up unit and using InfiniBand/Ethernet primarily between nodes, Grace
Blackwell NVL72 makes the entire rack the scale-up domain, integrating 18 one-U compute
trays—each with two 72-core Grace Arm CPUs and four Blackwell GPUs—plus nine
NVLink switch trays, management switches, , a 50–51V DC busbar, copper NVLink
cartridges and liquid manifolds, so that 36 CPUs and 72 GPUs operate as one low-latency

NVLink-connected system. Based on the same compute die, Nvidia upgraded the HBM
content to further improve the performance of GB300 chip. Overall, the rack level power
consumption is estimated at ~120KW for the GB200 NVL72 and ~140KW for the GB300
NVL72.
Vera Rubin—often called VR200/R200 in industry roadmaps—pushes the same rack-
level philosophy further. Nvidia’s disclosed architecture combines 72 dual-die Rubin GPUs
with 36 new Vera CPUs, replacing Grace’s 72 Arm Neoverse cores with 88 custom

Olympus cores, raising CPU memory capacity and bandwidth to as much as 1.5TB
LPDDR5X and 1.2TB/s, and doubling coherent CPU–GPU NVLink-C2C bandwidth to 1.8TB/
s; Rubin itself moves from HBM3e to 288GB HBM4 at up to 22TB/s, introduces a third-

### Page 22

M
Asia Pacific Insight
22
generation Transformer Engine and raises sixth-generation NVLink scale-up bandwidth to
3.6TB/s per GPU, while ConnectX-9 provides 1.6Tbps/GPU scale-out connectivity and
BlueField-4 increasingly offloads storage, security and infrastructure processing.
Nvidia has not published a simple Rubin chip TDP in its primary datasheet, but our supply
chain checks indicate roughly 1.8kW at an efficiency-oriented operating point and up to

approximately 2.3kW at the performance mode, which is consistently corroborated by
system vendors, with ASUS specifying 187kW Max-Q and 227kW Max-P for its fully liquid-
cooled Vera Rubin NVL72.
Looking ahead, we expect this trend to extend into the future along with the technology
advancement and more critical workloads.

Networking consuming more power as well
The network switch system is another key devices within the datacenter and has been
evolving  over the past years, driven primarily by advances in the switch ASIC. From
Tomahawk 1 (2014) to Tomahawk 6 (2025), the process node shrank from 28nm to 3nm,
while aggregate switching bandwidth increased from 3.2 Tbps to 102.4 Tbps, representing
a 32× increase. Over the same period, SerDes speed improved from 25 Gbps NRZ to 200

Gbps PAM4, enabling maximum port speeds to scale from 100G to 1.6T.
While process scaling significantly improved switching efficiency, it did not prevent ASIC
power from rising. ASIC TDP increased from 150 W to 800 W, reflecting the growing
complexity of higher radix switches and faster SerDes interfaces.
The impact of ASIC scaling is evident at the system level. As switch bandwidth increased
from 100G-class systems to 800G and 1.6T platforms, power delivery requirements grew

dramatically. Representative PSU ratings increased from 850W in Tomahawk 2 systems to
3,200W in Tomahawk 6 systems, nearly a 4× increase.
The rise in PSU power is driven not only by the higher ASIC TDP, but also by the growing
power consumption of high-speed optics, increased cooling requirements, and larger
overall system power budgets. As systems evolved from 100G to 1.6T ports, switch
vendors were forced to add more fan redundancy, larger heatsinks, and in some cases

larger chassis footprints. For example, Tomahawk 6 systems have expanded to 4U form
factors with 7+1 fan redundancy to accommodate the thermal load associated with 102.4
Tbps switching capacity.
Alongside the increasing power consumption of the server racks, network switches are
also witnessing higher power budgets  as well.

Power supply growing on both volume and specs
PSU spec upgrade trend
In response to the increasing power consumption of the AI servers, the power supply
architecture has been migrating as well. in the A100 and H100/200 single system servers,
6 power supply units are placed directly inside the chassis, with each capable to deliver
3.0-3.3KW of power.
When Nvidia make a broad based move to the rack level server solution from the

Blackwell generation, the power supply units were no longer placed directly within the
chassis housing the HPCs that consume power; instead, power supply units are separated
and grouped into power shelves. In the GB200/300 systems, there are 8 power shelves
sitting on the same rack as the compute trays and switch trays, each enclosing six 5.5KW
power supply units.
For the upcoming Vera Rubin rack, the power supply unit will be upgraded to 18.3KW of

output to comprise a power shelf of 110KW, and each power shelf will take 3U of space on
the rack vs. 1U in the Blackwell generation.
