Continuous margin expansion on favorable product mix
Delta's gross margin stayed largely between 28-29% during 2021-23, as revenue was
mainly driven by relatively lower-margin products, including EV- and consumer/IT-related

products, while server-related offerings had yet to scale up. 2024 served as an inflection
point, with the product mix changing structurally, supported by robust demand for AI data
center-related offerings, while its legacy businesses stayed relatively weak. Gross margin
thus grew by 3.2ppt YoY during the year, from 29.2% in 2023 to 32.4% in 2024. Increasing
AI computing needs further increased power consumption and cooling requirements per
rack, boosting demand for high-density AI power and cooling products along with related

system-level solutions in 2025, which lifted gross margin by another 1.8ppt YoY to 34.3%
for the year.
The opex ratio was largely stable, fluctuating around 18-19% between 2020 and 2023. It
then increased to 19% in 2023 and 21.1% in 2024, mainly due to operating deleverage,
higher R&D costs, and ongoing capacity expansion. The ratio declined back to 19.1% in
2025 on the back of an enlarged revenue base, despite R&D-related investments
remaining elevated.

Looking ahead, we expect gross margin to continue expanding on the back of increasing
revenue contribution from AI-related power supplies. We thus pencil in a 2.0ppt gross
margin expansion to 36.2% in 2026, followed by further increases of 1.3ppt and 0.5ppt
YoY in 2027 and 2028, respectively, reaching 38.0% by 2028. On the opex ratio, we
expect Delta to continue benefiting from operating leverage in the coming years, and thus
estimate operating margin to improve more substantially than gross margin, expanding by

2.7ppt YoY in 2026 to 17.8%, followed by further expansions of 2.8ppt and 1.5ppt in 2027
and 2028, reaching 20.6% and 22.1%, respectively.

Net profit to grow at a 63% CAGR in 2025-28
Delta's operating profit grew sequentially throughout 2020-22, supported by gross
margin expansion and a lower opex ratio, from NT$31,426mn in 2020 to NT$41,439mn in
2022. It then saw a slight 1% YoY decline in 2023 due to higher operating expenses as the
company further stepped up its R&D investments, despite a 0.4ppt gross margin
expansion in 2023. Operating margin also edged down accordingly by 0.6ppt to 10.2%

during the year.
Operating profit returned to a growth trajectory from 2024 onward, growing 16% YoY in
2024 and 76% YoY in 2025, as AI-related power offerings started to scale up. Operating
margin expanded faster than gross margin, supported by operating leverage and
disciplined cost controls, increasing by 1.1ppt in 2024 to 11.3% and another 3.8ppt to 15.1%
in 2025.
With the tax rate staying largely stable at ~20% during 2020-23 before starting to edge

up in 2024 and reaching 23% in 2025, Delta's net profit growth was largely aligned with
its operating profit growth trajectory. 2023 was an exception, as net profit still managed
to grow 2% YoY despite a 1% YoY decline in operating profit, mainly due to higher FX
gains.
The net margin trend has been largely aligned with that of operating margin in recent
years. After dropping from 9.0% in 2020 to 8.5% in 2021, as lower gross margin was
offset by a lower opex ratio, net margin hovered around the 8.3-8.5% level during 2021-

24. Net margin was largely flat YoY in 2024 despite a 1.1ppt expansion in operating margin,
mainly due to lower non-operating income and a slightly higher tax rate. We then saw a
surge in 2025, with net margin expanding 2.5ppt YoY to 10.8%, benefiting from a larger
contribution from higher-margin AI power offerings.
Looking ahead, we forecast net profit to grow 72% YoY in 2026e, followed by 63% YoY
growth in 2027e and 56% YoY growth in 2028e, implying a 63% CAGR in 2025-28e,

assuming a stable tax rate of 22%. Net margin is likely to rise accordingly, from 10.8% in
2025 to 13.3% in 2026, followed by further expansion to 14.9% in 2027 and 15.8% in 2028.

ROE to rise alongside margin expansion
ROE rose gradually but steadily during 2020-22, from 14.3% to 14.5%, mainly driven by
improving asset turnover, offset by lower net margin. ROE edged down to 13.8% in 2023
and fell further to 12.6% in 2024 on the back of lower asset turnover, while net margin

and financial leverage remained at similar levels. It then rebounded to 18.5% in 2025,
supported by both improving asset turnover and net margin expansion, which was fueled
by an increasingly favorable product mix and operating leverage.
Looking ahead, we expect ROE to continue rising, reaching 25.9% in 2026, 32.7% in 2027,
and 37.8% in 2028, primarily driven by continued margin expansion and improving asset
turnover. Net margin is projected to expand from 10.8% in 2025 to 15.8% in 2028,

supported by gross margin improvement—from 34.3% to 38.0%—and a declining
operating expense ratio from 19.1% in 2025 to 15.9% in 2028.

Improving balance sheet and continuous free cash flow
generation
Delta has generated positive operating cash flow in recent years. Operating cash flow
declined in 2021, mainly due to higher inventory buildup amid supply chain disruptions
during the year. Free cash flow generation has also remained positive, while fluctuating
with capital expenditures over the years. Free cash flow was exceptionally low at NT
$5,292mn in 2021, as the company started to step up capex mainly for geographic capacity

diversification and greater production flexibility—adding new capacity in Thailand, India,
Tainan/Zhongli in Taiwan, and Wuhu, Wujiang, and Chongqing in Mainland China. Both
absolute capex and the capex-to-sales ratio have trended upward since 2022, as Delta
continued to add capacity for EV-related offerings and power and cooling solutions during
2023-24, with further acceleration in 2025 due to capacity tightness for AI power and
cooling offerings, especially in Thailand and the US.

Looking ahead, the company aims to continue adding capacity worldwide to fulfill
substantial demand for power and liquid-cooling turnkey solutions for large-scale data
centers operated by major hyperscalers beyond 2027. Capacity remains tight for AI power
and cooling offerings, so it continues to expand actively in China, Thailand, the US, and
Tainan. Management also plans to add mass-production lines in Taoyuan, Taiwan,
scheduled to be ready by 2028, in addition to the pilot-run lines in Tainan. Accordingly, we

now estimate capex to rise to NT$70bn in 2026 from ~NT$46bn in 2025 and stay at a
similar level in 2027 before edging down slightly to NT$60bn in 2028.
Delta's net cash position has been rising since 2021, with its net cash-to-equity ratio
improving from just 2% in 2021 to 40% in 2025. We expect this ratio to continue rising,
reaching 51% in 2026, 57% in 2027, and 63% in 2028, on strong free cash flow generation
despite a higher total debt level amid ongoing capacity expansion.

Company Background –  Delta
Company snapshot
Delta Electronics is a global provider of power and thermal management technologies,
with a focus on energy-efficient products and integrated solutions. Founded in 1971, the
company serves a broad range of applications spanning components, power and system
products, thermal management, mobility, industrial and building automation, data centers,
telecom, and energy infrastructure, across industries including automotive, healthcare,

data centers, and power grids. By combining its capabilities in power electronics, digital
technologies, and system integration, Delta aims to support customers in improving
operational efficiency and advancing smart manufacturing, smart cities, and more
sustainable infrastructure.
The company is headquartered in Taipei, Taiwan. Its global network consists of 293
operations, including 165 sales offices, 55 manufacturing sites, and 73 R&D centers,

supporting customers worldwide.

Product offerings
Delta's key offerings can be categorized into four key areas: Power Electronics,
Infrastructure, Mobility, and Automation.
1. Power Electronics
Delta operates in switching power supplies, thermal management, and passive
components, supplying products for ICT, cloud computing, EVs, and industrial and
commercial applications. The company frames its business around energy efficiency and

### Page 77

M
Asia Pacific Insight
Morgan Stanley Research 77
sustainability and now also offers grid-to-chip power and thermal management solutions
for data centers.
• Components: Includes essential passive and magnetic components used in portable
devices, AI cloud computing, automotive electronics, and IoT.
• Power and System: Contains power products and systems for AI, cloud computing,
IoT, client devices, and industrial, medical, lighting, e-mobility, and electrical-

appliance applications.
• Fans and Thermal Management: This mainly covers air and liquid cooling solutions
for AI cloud and edge computing, EVs, as well as industrial, commercial, and
residential sectors. Key products includes DC and EC fans, thermal modules, liquid
cooling cold plates, and  coolant distribution units, etc.

2. Infrastructure
Delta supplies ICT and energy infrastructure to support demand related to AI, smart cities,
and IoT. Its ICT infrastructure solutions target data centers and telecom networks, while

its energy infrastructure offerings integrate AIoT, power conversion, and microgrid control
across energy generation, storage, and management.
• ICT Infrastructure: It mainly provides infrastructure for AI computing and 5G

### Page 78

M
Asia Pacific Insight
78
communications under this category, ranging from telecom cell sites to data
centers. The portfolio includes power, air and liquid cooling, networking
equipment, and management software.
• Energy Infrastructure: The company offers renewable energy, AIoT-based energy
management, and power conversion technologies for low-carbon transformation.
Solutions include solar inverters, wind power converters, energy storage, EV
charging, and management platforms for smart grids. It also supplies

semiconductor high-voltage power supplies, automated testing instruments, and
variable-frequency drives for industrial processes.
• Display and Visualization: Delta's display technologies serve control rooms, public
information systems, projection mapping, and exhibition or event spaces. Offerings
range from large-scale and 8K projection systems to integrated video-wall
solutions and can be customized for mission-critical operations.

3. Mobility
Delta applies its power electronics and motor technologies to smart mobility, supplying

### Page 79

M
Asia Pacific Insight
Morgan Stanley Research 79
power systems, drive technologies, and integrated powertrain solutions. It works with
automakers to develop electric vehicles, positioning the business as part of the low-
carbon transition and sustainable urban mobility.
• EV Powertrain System: The company's powertrain portfolio for EV makers includes
On-board Charging Modules (OBCM), DC/DC converters, power electronics
combos, and traction inverters, along with X-in-One integrated solutions.

4. Automation
Delta provides automation solutions for industrial and building applications through
integrated software and hardware. Its industrial automation offerings support smart

production and operational efficiency, while its building automation solutions target
energy-efficient, secure, and sustainable spaces.
• Industrial Automation: This segment provides automation solutions across
industries by utilizing AI, big data, IoT, and digitized manufacturing models.
Relevant offerings support diverse production needs, including customized, small-
volume and high-variety production, as well as mass production.
• Building Automation: Delta's Building Automation Solutions use an IoT

architecture and integrate technologies from five brands: Amerlux lighting, Delta
Controls and LOYTEC IP-based building control, and March Networks and
VIVOTEK surveillance. The management platform enables operators to manage
energy use, occupant comfort, and sustainability goals through a single interface.

Monthly sales vs. share price
Delta's monthly sales show that seasonality has been an important factor over the years.
Following a substantial increase in monthly sales since 2024, traditional seasonal patterns
have become less pronounced as revenue from AI-related offerings has started to
outweigh them, while liquid-cooling-related offerings have added a new layer of monthly

sales volatility. The correlation between the company's monthly sales and share price
movements has remained high, at ~87% since 2023.
The company reported August monthly sales of NT$64,586mn (-4% MoM/+35% YoY). We
saw the Power Electronics segment decline sequentially from a high base, offset by
growth in Automation and slight growth in the Infrastructure segment.

Key management & shareholding structure
Delta's board currently consists of eight directors and five independent non-executive
directors. Its founder, Chung-Hua Cheng (Bruce Cheng), and his sons, Ping Cheng and An
Cheng, together owned a 7% stake as of March 2026. Excluding the founder and his
family, the management team held a stake of ~0.1% as of March 2026.
The FINI holding percentage as of July 14, 2026, was 63%, versus a previous peak of 78% in

February 2011 and a historical average (since 2010) of 69%.

Valuation Methodology – Lite-On
Our price target (base case scenario value) is NT$315, derived from 17x 2027-28e P/E:
We use price/earnings as our valuation methodology because we believe it appropriately
captures Lite-On's earnings growth potential. We believe our target P/E multiple of 17x is
justified by an earnings CAGR of 47% over 2025-28, implying a PEG of 0.4. This is lower
than the average PEG of 7.2 in 2022-25, when the stock was trading at an average P/E of

16x for a 2% earnings CAGR.
Bull case scenario value – NT$500: We apply a 27x P/E multiple to our average base case
estimate for 2027-28. This scenario assumes better-than-expected margin expansion,
driven by improved operating efficiency and accelerating production automation, while
new business revenue comes in earlier than expected. Growth in cloud computing and
automotive electronics is stronger than expected.
Bear case scenario value – NT$200: We apply an 11x P/E multiple to our average base

case estimate for 2027-28. This scenario assumes weaker-than-expected improvement in
operating efficiency, while pricing competition intensifies amid macro volatility;
automotive electronics take longer to ramp up.

Global Footprint – Lite-On
More capacity expansion to come
Lite-On's production footprint spans the globe, with 15 sites in total, although it remains
heavily weighted toward China. The company has been expanding aggressively, both to
meet strong demand for AI power supplies and to support its strategy of geographic risk
diversification and proximity to customers.
Looking at its expansion plans in recent years, the company has continued to demonstrate

its commitment to diversifying production away from China and increasing proximity to
customers. It completed its Thailand plant expansion and announced the second-phase
expansion of its Vietnam facility in 2021. It then established automotive electronics and
cloud power production lines in Dallas, Texas, in 2H22, along with the official launch of
the second phase of its Hai Phong plant in Vietnam, which focuses on networking and
consumer products. In 2023, it began expanding its second Kaohsiung plant and

announced additional capacity expansion in Vietnam. In 2024, it committed a further US
$120mn to establish a wholly owned subsidiary in Vietnam and began progressively
shifting production of high-end power products to that facility. In 2025, the company
aimed to double capacity as demand for AI server power supplies and battery backup units
(BBUs) surged, focusing primarily on capacity expansion in the US, Kaohsiung, and
Vietnam.
For 2026, the company expects capacity allocation of ~40% in China, 27% in Vietnam,

20% in Taiwan, and 10% in the US, with the remaining non-China footprint (i.e., Mexico,
Brazil, and Thailand) accounting for ~3% of total capacity. It has also announced
investments of US$919mn and US$149mn in its US and Vietnam subsidiaries, respectively,
to continue expanding power-related capacity, mainly for PSUs. The capex budget for
2026 has also been raised to ~NT$13bn, versus ~NT$7bn in 2025.

Financial Analysis –  Lite-On
29% revenue CAGR in 2025-28
Lite-On's revenue growth was relatively moderate during 2020-22, with revenue
expanding at a CAGR of 5% over the period. The company benefited from surging cloud
computing demand, which drove increasing demand for power supplies, along with rising
demand from EV/automotive electronics, although growth was offset by elevated
component costs and supply-chain disruptions during COVID-19. Revenue then fell by 14%

in 2023 and 8% in 2024 due to a post-pandemic demand pullback across the consumer
electronics market and PC/NB model transitions, coupled with softening demand from EV-
related businesses. The IT segment declined 20%/11% YoY, while the opto-electronics
segment declined 11%/9% YoY in 2023/2024, respectively.
Revenue rebounded in 2025, growing 21% YoY, as the company began to benefit from
surging demand for AI/cloud server power supplies. Contributions from ramping high-

voltage/AI power infrastructure products began to increase, coupled with a partial
recovery in the IT segment, mainly fueled by power adapter shipments for game consoles
and gaming PCs/NBs, as well as related power offerings for low-Earth-orbit applications.
Looking ahead, we estimate revenue to expand at a 29% CAGR in 2025-28e, driven by the
AI server power demand boom, while we remain mindful of weakness in consumer/IT
demand amid elevated component costs.

By product segment, IT-related products were the primary revenue driver in past years,
while 2025 marked a crossover point, with the contribution from the Cloud & AIoT
segment surpassing that of the IT segment, mainly supported by AI power-related
offerings. IT revenue contribution declined from 72% in 2020 to 38% in 2025, and we
estimate it will reach 17% by 2028. For the Cloud & AIoT segment, revenue contribution
grew substantially from 27% in 2021 (0% in 2020) to 45% in 2025; we estimate it will

account for 74% of total revenue by 2028, on the back of surging AI power-related
demand. For opto-electronics, we expect revenue contribution to decline from 17% in
2025 to 9% in 2028.

Continuing margin expansion
Lite-On's gross margin rose steadily over the years, from 17.4% in 2020 to 22.0% in 2023,
thanks to a continually improving product mix. Gross margin dipped to 21.6% in 2024,
mainly due to a smaller revenue base, while contributions from AI/cloud had yet to scale

and margins were weighed down by learning-curve costs at the new Vietnam site. It then
rose to 22.9% in 2025 as the AI server power business began to scale alongside rising BBU
(battery backup unit) shipments, resulting in a more favorable product mix.
The opex ratio was largely stable at 10.5-11.0% during 2020-22. It then increased to 12.2%
in 2023 and 12.1% in 2024, mainly due to operating deleverage and higher R&D costs. The
opex ratio edged up further to 12.8% in 2025, mainly due to ongoing investment in R&D to

support new business development in AI server power supplies, BBUs, liquid cooling, etc.,
along with a one-off tariff prepayment.
Looking ahead, we expect margins to continue expanding on a favorable product mix. We
now pencil in a 2.1ppt gross margin improvement in 2026 to 25.0%, followed by
expansions of 1.3ppt and 1.1ppt in 2027 and 2028, to 26.3% and 27.4%, respectively. For
the opex ratio, we expect it to decline sequentially as the revenue base expands.

Accordingly, we estimate operating margin to improve by 3.3ppt to 13.4% in 2026,
followed by expansions of 1.7ppt in both 2027 and 2028, reaching 15.1% and 16.8%.

Net profit to grow at a 47% CAGR in 2025-28
Operating profit grew steadily alongside gross margin expansion during 2020-22, from NT
$10,206mn to NT$15,073mn. It then fell 4% YoY in 2023 and a further 11% YoY in 2024
due to higher operating expenses, mainly from R&D costs, despite gross margin expansion
in 2023. Operating margin increased by 1.1ppt YoY to 9.8% in 2023 amid a smaller revenue
base, before declining by 0.3ppt YoY to 9.5% in 2024 on lower gross margin and an

elevated opex ratio.
Operating profit rebounded by 29% YoY to NT$16,738mn in 2025, supported by both an
expanding revenue base and strengthening gross margin. Operating margin also improved,
increasing by 0.6ppt YoY to 10.1%, as the recovery in gross margin was partly offset by
higher opex.
With the tax rate remaining relatively stable at 20-24%, Lite-On's net profit growth has
generally tracked its operating profit growth. An exception occurred in 2023, when net

profit grew 3% YoY despite a 4% decline in operating profit, mainly due to higher FX gains
during the year.
Net margin has broadly tracked operating margin in recent years. It reached a record high
of 9.8% in 2023 on improving gross margin, despite a higher opex ratio. It then declined to
8.7% in 2024 on gross margin erosion before rebounding to 9.1% in 2025, benefiting from
increasing demand for AI power supplies.
Looking ahead, we forecast net profit to grow 67% YoY in 2026e, followed by 44% YoY

growth in 2027e and 32% YoY growth in 2028e, implying a 47% CAGR in 2025-28e. Net
margin is likely to rise to 11.2% in 2026, 12.2% in 2027, and 13.4% in 2028.

ROE to rise
ROE movements were highly correlated with changes in net margin during 2020-22, as
asset turnover and financial leverage were largely stable over the period. ROE edged
down slightly in 2023 despite higher net margin, which increased from 8.2% to 9.8%, due

to lower asset turnover and financial leverage. ROE fell further to a trough of 13.1% in
2024 as net margin and asset turnover declined further. It then rebounded to 16.8% in
2025, primarily supported by net margin expansion, fueled by a favorable product mix,
along with an improvement in asset turnover back to the 80% level.
Looking ahead, we expect ROE to continue rising, reaching 26.4% in 2026, 35.3% in 2027,
and 42.4% in 2028, primarily driven by continued margin expansion and improving asset

turnover. Net margin is projected to increase from 9.1% in 2025 to 13.4% by 2028,
supported by gross margin improvement from 22.9% to 27.4% and a decline in the
operating expense ratio from 12.8% in 2025 to 10.5% in 2028.

Improving balance sheet and continuous free cash flow
generation
Lite-On has consistently generated positive operating cash flow in recent years, although
cash flow dropped notably in 2021, 2024, and 2025, mainly due to higher working-capital-
related cash outflows. The company has also consistently generated positive free cash
flow, with capital expenditures generally at the NT$3-4bn level over the years. An
exception occurred in 2025, when capital expenditure rose to NT$6.7bn, mainly for power-

related capacity expansion in Vietnam, Taiwan, and the US.
As the company has announced investments of US$919mn and US$149mn in its US and
Vietnam subsidiaries, respectively, alongside continued expansion of power-related
capacity, mainly for PSUs, we now estimate capex to rise to ~NT$13bn in 2026, versus ~NT
$7bn in 2025. Capex should then decline to NT$9bn in 2027 as investment levels
normalize.
Lite-On's net cash position has remained relatively healthy, with its net cash-to-equity

ratio improving from 65% in 2020 to 80% in 2023. It then declined to 75% in 2024 and
66% in 2025, as total debt increased by NT$7.7bn in 2024, while cash on hand declined in
2025, likely due to increasing capital investment.
Looking ahead, we assume the total debt position will remain stable despite ongoing
capex investment. The net cash position should continue to grow sequentially on the back
of positive free cash flow generation.

Lite-On's average capex-to-sales ratio was around 2.3-2.4% during 2021-24, then rose to
4.1% in 2025 as the company began aggressively expanding global capacity, despite a

larger revenue base during the year. Looking ahead, we project the capex-to-sales ratio to
rise to 5.9% in 2026 amid continued production-site development, before declining to 3.0-
3.3% in 2027-28 as investments normalize.

The depreciation-to-sales ratio remained at 2.4-2.5% during 2020-22 before rising to 2.8%

revenue base grows faster than capex and depreciation, falling to 1.6% in 2026 and 1.0% in

Lite-On's cash conversion cycle (CCC) fluctuated within a relatively narrow band of
roughly 35-44 days between 2020 and 2025, reflecting generally stable but modestly
shifting working-capital efficiency. CCC was at a relatively low level of 35 days in 2020,

supported by lower inventory days and higher AP days, before rising steadily to 44 days in
2022 as both inventory days (78) and AR days (85) increased, while AP days were slightly
lower (119). From 2023 onward, the cycle improved meaningfully, falling to 36 days as AP
days extended sharply from 119 in 2022 to 135. This improvement partially reversed in
2024, with CCC ticking up to 39 days as inventory and AR days both increased to 89 and
92 days, respectively, even as AP days extended further to 143. CCC then eased again to 37

days in 2025 as AP days moderated slightly to 138, alongside stable inventory and
receivables levels.
Looking ahead, we expect the overall cash conversion cycle to edge up slightly from 37
days in 2025 to 40 days in 2028, as increasing AR days and lower AP days are partially
offset by improving inventory days.

Company Background –  Lite-On
Company snapshot
Founded in 1975, Lite-On Technology is a Taiwan-based manufacturer of optoelectronic
components, power supplies, and information technology products. The company
produced Taiwan's first LEDs and became the first technology company listed on the
Taiwan Stock Exchange in 1983. It mainly operates through four business segments:
Information Products (power supplies, servers, networking equipment, keyboards, and

chassis), Optoelectronics (LED packaging, LED lighting, automotive electronic
components, and camera modules), Storage (storage components), and other supporting
businesses. Additional product lines include energy management solutions, such as data
center power supply systems and cloud server chassis; networking and surveillance
products; and automotive electronics, including ADAS and EV charging solutions.
Lite-On is headquartered in Taipei, Taiwan. The company operates manufacturing sites,

R&D centers, and sales offices across Asia (Taiwan, China, Japan, and Singapore), the US,
and Europe (the UK, the Netherlands, and Germany).

Product offerings
Lite-On's key offerings can be categorized into six key areas: Optoelectronic
Semiconductors, Energy Management, Consumer Electronics, Networking and
Surveillance, Automotive Electronics, and Roadway Intellimation System.
1. Optoelectronic semiconductors
Its product portfolio spans a broad technological range, encompassing illumination and
sensing applications, low- to high-power devices, standard and custom-made solutions,
and components ranging from LEDs to lasers and from visible light to infrared and

ultraviolet spectra.
Exhibit 144:Lite-on: Offerings for optoelectronic semiconductors

Source: Company data, Morgan Stanley Research
2. Energy management

The product range serves the communications, industrial automation, smart home,
medical, office, and automotive electronics industries. It offers a comprehensive suite of
platinum- to titanium-efficiency-grade power supplies (CRPS, GPU, and SLIM series)
suitable for both standard and AI servers, supporting AC or DC input with high power
density, high reliability, and modular scalability. Power-shelf and battery-backup-unit
(BBU) solutions, along with rack-cooling and liquid-cooling technologies for data centers,

cloud infrastructure, and AI/machine-learning platforms, are also included in this segment.

5. Automotive electronics

### Page 101

Asia Pacific Insight
Morgan Stanley Research 101
Lite-On is able to leverage its core competencies in optoelectronics and power
management, along with more than four decades of expertise in automotive electronics.
This segment is dedicated to advancing vehicle-charging technology, ADAS (Advanced
Driver Assistance Systems) solutions, and automotive lighting modules through high-end
engineering. AC charging solutions are offered across multiple current ratings (32/40A,

48A, 80A, and an EU-specific variant), while DC charging solutions include a 30kW fast-
charging offering.

6. Roadway Intellimation System

This segment is mainly operated under the LEOTEK brand, a subsidiary of the LITEON
Technology Group with dual headquarters in Silicon Valley and Taipei. It has served road
infrastructure across more than 30 countries—including North America, Europe, the
Middle East, and the Asia-Pacific region—since 1992. The segment comprises intelligent
roadway lighting, traffic signal systems, and AI-driven predictive maintenance solutions, all
developed to support smarter and more efficient road infrastructure.

Monthly sales vs. share price
Lite-On's monthly sales have hovered around the NT$10-20bn level since 2013. Notably,
we see a high correlation of ~76% between the company's monthly sales and its share
price movements since 2023.
The company reported August monthly sales of NT$19,592mn (+3% MoM/+25% YoY).

Key management & shareholding structure
Lite-On's board currently consists of five directors and four independent non-executive
directors.
Its founder, Raymond Soong, and current Chairman, Tom Soong, together owned a stake
of ~5% as of June 2026. Excluding the founder and Chairman, the management team

owned a stake of ~0.1% as of June 2026.
The FINI holding percentage as of July 7, 2026, was 41%, versus a previous peak of 62% in
April 2017 and a historical average (since 2010) of 46%.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Panasonic Holdings (6752.T)
Our target price is based on P/E 19.5x our FY3/28 EPS forecast.
We apply: 36x to EPS generated by AI infrastructure-related businesses, 5x to EPS attribu-
table to IRA subsidies in the automotive battery business, and 13.5x to EPS from all other
businesses.
Risks to Upside
n Acceleration of restructuring and exit/scale-down of unprofitable businesses
n Stronger than expected growth of datacenter BBU, automotive battery  business

Risks to Downside
n Deteriorating visibility for energy business
n Expectations for business portfolio revisions sink

12-18 months.

Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

營收走勢圖

研究員/莊佳蓉 EVA8894122@uni-psg.com

研究部維持奇鋐買進投資建議，並上修目標價，主係：(1) 4Q26 隨著
VR200 小量出貨，AWS T3 水冷及Google TPU 同步放量，營收與獲利
將逐季走高；(2)預期 AWS 及 AMD 將採用雙面水冷版，單價大幅提升；
(3)ASIC 產品毛利率高於GPU， 且預估27 年水冷營收比重將超過 5 成；
(4) NVIDIA CDU 認證名單及 Switch tray 規格的市場雜音，對基本
面影響有限。綜上所述，研究部上修 27 年營收至 3,409 億元(原估
3,144 億元)，EPS 上修至 180.69 元(原估 162.71 元)，給予 25 倍本
益比，目標價上修至 4,500 元，維持買進建議。

重點摘要： 
1. 4Q隨著VR小批量出貨，兩家ASIC放量，營運持續季增 
隨著GB300出貨持續強勁 ，VR小批量出貨， 研究部預期奇鋐9月
營收持續月增。展望4Q26，除了VR200板端開始小量出貨外，
Google TPU以及AWS T3水冷也將在4Q開始陸續放量，研究部微
幅調整4Q26營收至632億元(原估622億元)，季增11.9%，年增
32.3%。而VR200的出貨將持續到2Q27，3Q27將開始有小量Rubin 
ultra產品接續。

2. AWS及AMD採用雙面水冷板，散熱產值增加 
根據供應鏈調查， AWS T3水冷以及AMD MI系列將採用雙面式
水冷板，主因採用背面供電(主板空間不夠，記憶體模組移到
主板背面，故兩面都需水冷板散熱)，雙面式水冷板單價將有
望超過3000美元，產值大幅提升。此外，ASIC產品毛利率優於
GPU，公司產能也優先配置給ASIC，故研究部上修27年營收至
3409億元。

3. CDU認證及switch tray規格雜音，對基本面影響極小 
近期散熱有部分雜音：(1) NVIDIA DSX Ready 的CDU認證裡，
取得資格的業者為 LG Electronics、LiquidStack與Vertiv三
間，奇鋐未在首批名單中。研究部認為，目前奇鋐目前主力產
品仍為水冷板等零組件，CDU比較偏向系統級產品 ，27-28年才
會開始小量生產CDU ( 以In row 架構為主)，但目前營收比重
低，影響不大。(2)傳言27年Rubin Ultra 的Switch tray將從
18層改回9層設計，目前仍在討論中，但研究部 對奇鋐的27年
EPS預估，本身就未納入18層規格。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

4. 上修27年EPS預估，目標價上修至4500元  
除了GPU及ASIC需要水冷板之外，周圍相關產品也開始逐漸導
入液冷，如CPU/LPU Rack 、CPO等，奇鋐預估27年水冷營收比
重將超過5成。綜上所述 ，研究部微幅上修2026年營收至2,178
億元(原估2,168億元)， 年增56.0%；EPS 104.65元(原估104.02
元)，並上修2027年營收至3,409億元(原估3,144億元)，年增
56.5%，EPS上修至180.69元(原估162.71元)，給予25倍本益比 ，
目標價上修至4,500元，維持買進建議。

告 中 所 有 的 意 見 及 預 估 ， 如 有 變 更 恕 不 另 行 通 知 。

供 專 屬 之 投 資 建 議 。 文 中 所 載 資 訊 或 任 何 意 見 ， 不 構 成 任 何 買 賣 有 價 證 券 或 其 他 投
資 標 的 之 要 約 、 宣 傳 或 引 誘 等 事 項 。 對 於 本 投 資 報 告 所 討 論 或 建 議 之 任 何 證 券 、 投
資 標 的 ， 或 文 中 所 討 論 或 建 議 之 投 資 策 略 ， 投 資 人 應 就 其 是 否 適 合 本 身 財 務 狀 況 與
投 資 條 件 ， 進 一 步 諮 詢 財 務 顧 問 的 意 見 。 本 投 資 報 告 之 內 容 取 材 自 據 信 為 可 靠 之 資

陳 述 或 保 證 。 本 投 資 報 告 載 述 意 見 進 行 更 改 與 撤 回 並 不 另 行 通 知 。 本 投 資 報 告 並 非

公 司 ， 為 於 該 管 轄 區 內 從 事 證 券 經 紀 或 交 易 之 遊 說 。 本 投 資 報 告 內 容 屬 統 一 投 顧 之

休閒車新訂單與電子新產品挹注成長 
 2H26 持續受惠美國 Honda 新訂單放量出貨 ， 以及越南電動機車客戶
產能供不應求的外包訂單需求 ， 營收可望逐季墊高 。 推估下半年平均
每月營收 8~8.5 億元。 
 休閒車產品 2026 年在客戶 Polaris 調整告一段落以及北美 Honda 與
哈雷新訂單開始挹注下，預期營收貢獻將成長 50%以上，2027 年北
美 Honda 還有其他車型將量產，推估可再成長 20-30%。 
 相關機櫃與散熱產品將於 2Q26 陸續少量出貨 ，2027 年將放量出貨 。
預估電子產品營收佔比年底可增至 3-5%。因應電子與大型自動化產
線需求越南百善二廠二期擴建，預計 3Q27 末完工，推估 2027 年營
收比重可達近雙位數。 
 展望 2026 年，受惠越南市場受惠發展電動二輪政策，以及受惠北美
Honda 與哈雷新訂單挹注。預期 2026 年 EPS 17.25 元。2027 年休
閒車新訂單挹注以及電子產品隨著產能開出放量 ， 預期EPS 21.3 元。 
 維持買進 ：公司受惠北美 HONDA 新訂單與電動機車客戶轉單挹
注，今明兩年營運轉好。此外，受惠供應鏈由中國轉移至越南，公司
越南廠具在地優勢取得電子新訂單 ， 隨著越南百善二廠廠能開出 ， 將
挹注未來成長動能。維持買進建議，目標價 234 元。

元富證券投資顧問股份有限公司 台北市敦化南路二段 97 號 19 樓 (02)2325-3299  (115)金管投顧新字第 016 號         Page 2 of 5 
個股報告

北美新訂單挹注，營運逐季墊高

越南電動二輪政策需求， 彌補燃
油機車缺口

公司 2Q26 營收 24.1 億元 ，+16.9%QoQ，+53.1%YoY， 主要為休閒車輛訂單回溫 ，
加上越南機車業務拉貨回升。在稼動率提升與產品結構轉好下，毛利率23.8%較上
季好轉，單季 EPS 4.42 元。

2H26 持續受惠美國 Honda 新訂單放量出貨 ， 以及越南電動機車客戶產能供不應求
的外包訂單需求 ， 營收可望逐季墊高 。 推估下半年平均每月營收8~8.5 億元 ，3Q26
營收 24.34 億元，+1%QoQ，+58.8%YoY，EPS 4.72 元。4Q26 為越南機車產業
旺季，預計營收 25.07 億元，+3%QoQ，+38.9%YoY，EPS 4.53 元。

1H26 主要產品營收佔比依序為機車 44.8%、 休閒車43.9%、 健康8.6%、 汽車2.6%。

機車產品方面，越南傳統機車市場為主，主要客戶為 YAMAHA 與 Honda，2025
年受到 HONDA 下修生產計劃影響，營收貢獻較前一年下滑 14%。2026 年越南河
內與胡志明市將禁止傳統燃油二輪，轉為發展電動二輪市場，雖影響傳統燃油機車
市場，但受惠政策，新的電動二輪換車需求彌補了油車下滑的缺口。  
目前客戶 Vinfast 產能吃緊釋出訂單 ，1H26 機車產品營收貢獻成長 22%， 預期2026
年機車營收可成長 16-17%。2027 年越南 Honda 將有電動二輪產品推出，有利維
持機車產品成長。

北美 Honda 與哈雷為休閒車產品
主要成長動能

汽車與健康事業轉佳

休閒車：主要客戶為 Polaris、Harley 與北美 Honda，受惠客戶為降低成本與避開
當地關稅的策略，將產線移至越南，以及美國當地非紅供應鏈政策，相關供應鏈由
中國轉向越南。而 2025 年在川普總統推動美國製造下，公司調整產線並於美國南
卡羅來納州設廠因應。2025 年受到通膨影響北美休閒車市場需求趨緩，主要客戶
Polaris 調整庫存，目前庫存水位已將至低位，自 1Q26 客戶陸續補庫存。

新訂單方面，北美 Honda ATV 零件，目前接單規劃至 2028 年都有新專案出貨。
由於 Honda ATV 在北美規模與 Polaris 相當，為未來成長的主要客戶與動能，新
訂單於 2Q26 量產，預估營收貢獻將從 2025 年的 4.9 億元大幅增至 2026 年的 
12~13 億元。此外，公司哈雷訂單於 1Q26 開始出貨，推估 2026 年可貢獻 9-10
億元，目前仍有新項目在開發，將挹注未來營運動能。

休閒車產品 2026 年在客戶 Polaris 調整告一段落以及北美 Honda 與哈雷新訂單開
始挹注下，預期營收貢獻將成長 50%以上。2027 年除了北美 Honda SXS 與 ATV
訂單外，還有其他車型將量產，加上哈雷，與歐洲、日本的新訂單，推估休閒車營
收貢獻可再成長 20-30%。

汽車相關產品取得 Toyota、Honda 與 Vinfast 電動車訂單，加上其他車廠未來也
規劃將組裝線設於越南， 公司看好未來越南與東協車用市場成長， 透過與相關供應
鏈的台商合資取得供應商資格，未來將可從業外認列轉投資收益。 2025 年受到越
南需求不如預期影響 ， 營收貢獻僅成長6%，2026 年越南車用市場回穩 ， 加上Vinfast

電子產品布局陸續開花結果

預期 2026 年 EPS 17.25 元，2027
年 21.3 元

維持買進

風險提示 
需求增加，營收貢獻成長 45%。

健康事業目前包含醫療、健身器材、E-bike 等產品，先前健身器材新產品雖取得客
戶認證，但出貨時間遞延，2025 年營收貢獻下滑 17-18%，目前開始小量出貨，
在基期較低下，預期 2026 年營收貢獻成長 25%。

公司於越南設立子公司，將所有電子相關客戶集中生產。產品為伺服器機櫃、散熱
產品與伺服器內部零部件，相關機櫃與散熱產品將於 2Q26 陸續少量出貨，2027
年將放量出貨。預估電子產品營收佔比年底可增至 3-5%。因應電子與大型自動化
產線需求越南百善二廠二期擴建，預計 3Q27 末完工，推估 2027 年營收比重可達
近雙位數。

主要產品 營收比重與 成長性預估

Source: ：Company Data 、Masterlink

展望 2026 年，機車產品受惠越南市場受惠發展電動二輪政策，而休閒車客戶
Polaris 庫存調整後開始補庫存，以及受惠北美 Honda 與哈雷新訂單挹注。預期
2026 年營收 94.13 億元，+38.3%YoY，EPS 17.25 元。2027 年休閒車新訂單挹
注以及電子產品隨著產能開出放量 ， 預期營收114.72 億元 ，+21.9%YoY，EPS 21.3
元。

公司受惠北美 HONDA 新訂單與電動機車客戶轉單挹注，今明兩年營運轉好。此
外，受惠供應鏈由中國轉移至越南，公司越南廠具在地優勢取得電子新訂單，隨著
越南百善二廠廠能開出，將挹注未來成長動能。維持買進建議，目標價 234 元。

營收走勢圖

研究員/張均壕 JUN_HAO1@uni-psg.com

由於北美 Honda 新車型訂單貢獻顯著，帶動 3Q26 營收淡季不淡，加上
電子新業務的訂單開始貢獻，為豐祥下半年營運挹注強勁成長動能， 研
究部據此上修豐祥今年獲利 8%，預估2026 年 EPS 17.17 元(+41%YOY)。
展望 2027 年，預期在休閒車輛、電動機車新訂單以及電子新業務貢獻
下，豐祥明年業績將繼續攀高，預估 2027 年 EPS 21.09 元。研究部給
予 12 倍本益比，將目標價上調至 250 元，維持買進評等。

重點摘要：

1. 電子業務啟動第二成長曲線，2027年進入量產階段： 
豐祥將電子業務視為企業新一輪發展的核心驅動力 ， 當前以AI
伺服器機櫃為主力產品以及其他電子相關品項。 今年下半年已
陸續有新產品少量出貨。在營收比重部分，2026年電子業務約
占合併營收的3~5%。未來隨著客戶需求釋放及新項目量產，搭
配新廠房啟用，創造更多生產空間，公司規畫2H27開始以大規
模自動化模式提升產能， 預期2027年電子業務將有更多的營收
貢獻，更能增添中長期營運動能。。

2. 3Q26淡季不淡，4Q26營收再攀高，年度獲利上修： 
豐祥第三季繼續受惠北美Honda新專案出貨順暢，季度營收 表
現優於原先季減的預期， 呈現淡季不淡 ， 延續前季的強勁表現 ，
年增率保持大幅成長 ； 之後進入越南當地電動機車零件拉貨旺
季，第四季營收有望再度衝高。整體而言，在休閒車輛與電動
機車業務雙引擎帶動下，公司下半年營收動能無虞，本業獲利
維持在高檔區域並保有顯著的年成長動能 ， 加上轉投資事業獲
利成長，研究部上修豐祥今年度獲利9%，預估豐祥2026年合併
營收94.68億元(+39%YOY)，毛利率23.4%(+1.0pct)，費用率
9.4%(+0.8pct)， 預估稅後淨利11.94億元(+41%YOY)， 預估2026
年EPS 17.17元。

3. 三大成長引擎接棒，2027年業績將再創高： 
展望2027年，研究部認為豐祥將繼續保有偏強的營運動能，主
要來自於三大核心領域 ：(1)休閒車輛新訂單持續堆疊與放量 。
特別是受惠於北美供應鏈轉移效益 ， 豐祥對客戶的供應滲透率
提升，北美Honda、Harley-Davidson等客戶陸續有新機種將於
明年量產；亦有日系客戶的新訂單將提前至明年出貨，使休閒
車業務在2027年依然是貢獻整體營收增長的最主要基石；

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

(2)越南當地電動機車與電動汽車市場規模擴大，相關訂單持
續增加，為既有業務提供成長支撐 ；(3)電子新業務的實質貢
獻。豐祥切入伺服器機櫃供應鏈，經過2026年下半年的試產準
備後，2027年將迎來訂單放量，預期電子業務佔營收比重躍升
至5%以上。此外，休閒車輛占比提高，產品組合將繼續優化，
毛利率能再墊高；配合物流逐漸回歸自營管理，費用率亦有望
下降，將進一步提升盈利能力。研究部預估豐祥2027年合併營
收116.46 億元(+23%YOY) ，毛 利 率23.7%(+0.3pct) ，費 用 率
9.1%(-0.3pct)， 預估稅後淨利14.70億元(+23%YOY)， 預估2027
年EPS 21.09元。

3Q26/4Q26 ABF、BT 稼動率持續向上，預期帶動營運成長 
南電 3Q26 主要成長動能來自於 ABF/BT，ABF 受惠 1.6T 高階交換器晶片及
AI ASIC 專案陸續放量，高階產品規格升級亦帶動平均銷售單價提升，其中
1.6T 交換器載板 ASP 相較前一代 800G 產品高出一倍以上。BT 載板則持續
受惠記憶體需求強勁。樹林廠及錦興廠的 ABF 與 BT 載板產能均已接近全面
滿載，昆山 ABF 稼動率 60%–70%以上，進入 4Q26 有望趨於滿載。本中心
預估 3Q/4Q26 營收 163.8/188.5 億元，季增 20.6%/15.1% ，毛利率
30%/32.3%，稅後獲利 35/46.3 億元，EPS 5.43/7.16 元。 
玻纖布、CCL 持續漲價，預期 4Q26–2027 年載板價格上漲 
經本中心供應鏈訪查了解，高階 BT 材料已呈明顯供不應求，使部分 BT 料
件 Lead time 甚至拉長至 70 週以上；ABF 隨新世代 AI 載板朝 20 層以上
Build-up 結構發展，單顆 ABF 膜使用量同步提高，Ajinomoto 供應亦趨緊；
玻纖布方面， Nittobo 新增玻纖布供給已大致完成客戶分配，短期缺口難以
明顯緩解。隨單顆載板面積放大，每張 Panel 可排入的產品數量將快速下降，
持續消耗載板廠商有效產能。綜合來看，本輪載板漲價由規格升級、單顆材
料用量增加與高階有效產能不足共同驅動，預期 ABF/BT 載板於 4Q26 至
2027 年仍具持續漲價空間。 
2027 年營運動能明顯增強，給予買進評等，目標價 1,700 元 
南電於 8M26 董事會通過 468 億元資本支出計畫，主要鎖定大尺寸、高層數、
高精密度 IC 載板。在高階產品比重提升、稼動率維持高檔及報價上調帶動
下，預期 ABF/BT 毛利率可望持續改善，整體營運動能於 2027 年明顯增強。
本中心估計 2026/2027 年南電營收 599.8/1,095.4 億元，年增 49.3%/82.6%，
毛利率 26.9%/43.4%，EPS 18.11/56.78 元。本中心給予南電買進評等，以
2027 年 EPS，30 倍本益比推得目標價 1,700 元。

元大觀點 
⧫  
台灣載板產能接近全面滿載，昆山 ABF 稼動率提升至 60%–70%且
4Q26 有望滿載，預估 3Q/4Q26 營收季增 20.6%/15.1%。 
⧫  
規格升級與高階 產品及上游原料 產能不足共同驅動載板漲價，預期
ABF/BT 載板於 4Q26 至 2027 年仍具持續漲價空間。 
⧫  
高階產品比重提升、稼 動率維持高檔及報價上調，給予 南電 買進評
等，目標價 1,700 元，以 2027 年 EPS 56.78 元及 30 倍本益比推估。

營運分析 
3Q26 ABF、BT 稼動率持續向上，預期帶動營運成長 
南電 3Q26 營收 163.8 億元，季增 20.6%，年增 49.3%，主要成長動能仍來自於 ABF/BT，ABF 載
板方面，受惠 1.6T 高階交換器晶片及 AI ASIC 專案陸續放量，除出貨量持續增加外，高階產品規
格升級亦帶動平均銷售單價提升。其中，1.6T 交換器載板 ASP 相較前一代 800G 產品高出一倍以
上，隨高階產品占比提升，為營收與獲利成長的推力。BT 載板則持續受惠 DRAM 與 NAND Flash
等記憶體需求強勁，加上上游原材料成本上漲逐步反映於產品報價，使 BT 載板呈現量價齊揚。毛
利率預估為 30%，營業利益 43.7 億元，季增 50.6%，年增 604.1%，稅後獲利 35 億元，季增 55%，
年增 383.6%，EPS 5.43 元。

台灣廠全面滿載，昆山 ABF 稼動率快速回升 
產能利用率方面，目前台灣主要載板廠區維持高檔運作，其中樹林廠及錦興廠的 ABF 與 BT 載板產
能均已接近全面滿載，顯示高階載板及記憶體相關需求仍相當強勁。同時，昆山 ABF 廠則為後續
營運改善的重要來源。隨美系網通大廠新專案自 7M26 正式進入量產，昆山 ABF 稼動率已由 2Q26
約 50%快速提升至 60%–70%，進入 4Q26 有望趨於滿載。伴隨稼動率提高及良率改善，昆山廠毛
利率亦顯著提升，預期後續貢獻幅度將提升。PCB 業務，稼動率正由 70%–75%向上，主因 AI 伺
服器板與記憶體模組板需求驅動。綜合上述，預期 4Q26 營收 188.5 億元，季增 15.1%，年增
68.8%，毛利率 32.3%，營業利益 55.1 億元，季增 26.1%，年增 472.1%，稅後獲利 46.3 億元，
季增 32%，EPS 7.16 元

資料來源：公司資料、元大投顧預估、Bloomberg

玻纖布、 CCL 等價格仍持續漲價，預期 4Q26-2027 年載板價格上漲 
隨 AI GPU 、ASIC、高速交換器及 HBM 等應用快速成長，IC 載板規格持續朝大尺寸、高層數、細
線路、低翹曲及高耐熱發展，帶動材料由一般電子級產品轉向特種玻纖布、高階 BT 樹脂、ABF 增
層膜等高規材料。經本中心近期供應鏈訪查及日本材料展資訊整理，三菱瓦斯化學（ MGC）指出，
過去 BT 載板主要應用於 DRAM、NAND Flash 及消費性 IC，但隨 HBM、AI ASIC 、網通晶片與
CPO 導入，材料須進一步滿足低 CTE、高耐熱及高速電性需求，高階 BT 與傳統產品的規格差距
持續擴大，目前高階 BT 材料已呈明顯供不應求，部分料件 Lead time 甚至拉長至 70 週以上。玻纖
布方面，Nittobo 目前產能維持 100%滿載，雖規劃 2027–2028 年新增產能，但新增供給已大致完
成客戶分配，短期缺口難以明顯緩解。ABF 方面，隨新世代 AI 載板朝 20 層以上 Build-up 結構發
展，單顆 ABF 膜使用量同步提高，Ajinomoto 供應亦趨緊；同時 Resonac 相關載板材料報價已調
升約 20%–30%。綜合來看，本輪載板漲價並非單純景氣循環，而是規格升級、單顆材料用量增加
與高階有效產能不足共同驅動，預期 ABF／BT 載板於 4Q26 至 2027 年仍具持續漲價空間。

### Page 4

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：PCB 2026 年 10 月 5 日 Nan Ya PCB 第4 頁，共 19頁  
圖 3：載板面積及層數增加亦在 Ajinomoto 未來載板發展藍圖

資料來源：Ajinomoto，元大投顧整理

南電此前已宣布 468 億元資本支出，為創立以來單筆最大投資 
南電於 8M26 董事會通過新一輪資本支出計畫，總投資金額達 468 億元，為公司成立以來最大單筆
投資案，規模亦超過 2018–2022 年上一輪擴產期間累計資本支出。其中約 110 億元投入廠房土建，
358 億元用於設備採購，新廠定位為具備 Class 100 無塵室等級的高階智慧工廠，主要鎖定大尺寸、
高層數、高精密度 IC 載板，以及 CoWoS、CoPoS 等先進封裝衍生需求。顯示此次投資並非既有
產線單純擴充，而是針對下一世代高階載板重新建置生產平台。整體而言 ，廠區調配方面，錦興廠
P2 升級已於 1H26 完成，主要提升自動化程度與無塵室規格，以因應大型、高階載板生產；樹林
廠則因應 2027 年美系客戶 Server CPU 需求成長，陸續將部分既有成熟產品轉往昆山生產，以釋
放台灣高階產能供新產品使用。

展望 2026-2027 年，ABF/BT 料源及載板的產能供應缺口將推升載板廠商營運 
AI 需求正在從「訓練」往「推論」延伸，這會讓晶片種類變多、數量變大，進而同時推升 IC 載板
的量與規格。此前從 Deep Learning 、Perceptual AI 一路到 Generative AI、Agentic AI，未來再往
Physical AI 發展。訓練（Learning）主要依賴大型模型運算，因此核心晶片以高效能 GPU 為主；
但到了推論（Inference）階段，更重視即時反應速度、功耗與成本，因此除了 GPU 之外，CPU 與
ASIC 的使用比例會明顯提高。展望 2026/2027 年，預計南電載板稼動率持續改善，其中 ABF 與
BT 均已進入高檔運作。ABF 方面，台灣樹林及錦興廠稼動率已接近滿載；昆山廠則為 2H26 稼動
率回升的主要來源，受惠美系網通客戶及中國客戶量產，ABF 稼動率由 2Q26 約 50%快速提升至
3Q26 的 60%–70%，預期 4Q26 在供給持續吃緊下，可進一步提升，2027 年初亦將因應美系客戶
Server CPU 需求快速成長。BT 載板供需則更加緊張，除現有 DRAM、SSD 相關需求持續強勁，
另一方面，部分同業逐步將 BT 產能轉向 ABF，韓系供應商亦優先支應集團內需求，市場有效供給
持續收縮，景碩亦表示稼動率也將逐步向上，預期 2027 年 BT 仍將維持高稼動率。 
綜合上述，本中心估計 2026/2027 年南電營收 599.8/1095.4 億元，年增 49.3%/82.6%，毛利率
26.9%/43.4%，營業利益 141.3/442.3 億元，年增 612.7%/213.1%，稅後獲利 117/366.9 億元，年
增 501.2%/213.5%，EPS 18.11/56.78 元。

### Page 5

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：PCB 2026 年 10 月 5 日 Nan Ya PCB 第5 頁，共 19頁  
產業概況  
AI 需求增溫造成 T-glass 玻纖布供應吃緊，助益近期 BT 載板報價調整 
低熱膨脹係數（Low CTE ）玻璃纖維布，簡稱 T-glass，為高階 AI ABF 載板及高階 BT 載板重要原
料，由 Nittobo(日東紡)獨家供應，並由日系廠商 Resonac(ABF 載板 CCL 主要供應商)、三菱瓦斯
(BT 載板 CCL 主要供應商)採用。由於高階 AI 晶片需求增溫帶動高階 AI ABF 載板出貨成長，造成
T-glass 供應吃緊並連帶使高階 BT 載板 CCL(終端用途包含記憶體控制器、手機 Ap、Sip 等)交貨
期拉長，T-glass 玻纖布供應正常時高階 BT 載板 CCL 交貨期約 8-10 週，後因供應吃緊造成交貨期
拉長至 16-20 週，目前因玻纖布供應更為緊張已無告知交貨期。 
經供應鏈調查，台系載板廠已於 2025 年 7 月開始針對部分採用 T-glass 之 BT 載板產品調漲價格，
此外 BT 載板亦有載板廠進行價格調漲，以反應高階玻纖布供應日漸緊缺，以及年初以來銅價、金
價等原物料成本上漲壓力。由於 BT 載板產業長期以來供過於求，且當前高階 AI 晶片普遍採用
ABF 載板，多數載板廠此前 BT 業務毛利率較為偏低，淡季時季毛利率多有承壓。本中心認為漲價
趨勢可延續，將有助載板廠獲利改善。

圖 4：高階 BT 載板供應鏈示意圖

資料來源：元大投顧整理

圖 6：金價近一年走勢

資料來源：Cmoney、元大投顧整理

中長期高階 ABF 載板價格或能進一步調升 
受惠 AI 發展浪潮帶動 AI GPU 、AI ASIC 等 AI 晶片需求成長，AI ABF 載板需求正向。本中心推測
短期高階 AI ABF 載板因獲利水準足夠暫無調漲壓力，但中長期隨需求持續上升、載板面積增大、
T-glass 供應持續吃緊，AI ABF 載板產品台系廠商後續仍有進一步議價空間。目前欣興、南電、景
碩皆有耕耘高階 ABF 載板，例如欣興在美系 GPU Blackwell 市佔率預估為 30-40%，且為全球主要
AI ASIC 載板供應商；南電今年 800G 載板比重預期約 7%，包含美系、非美系等終端需求；景碩
則有望成為美系 AI GPU 載板第三供。此外部分載板廠因原物料上漲等成本壓力在 2Q25、3Q25 小
幅調整中低階 ABF 載板報價，本中心判斷若 T-glass 供應持續吃緊並排擠 E-glass 產能，則後續中
低階 ABF 載板價格有望進一步提升。

### Page 7

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：PCB 2026 年 10 月 5 日 Nan Ya PCB 第7 頁，共 19頁  
AI、ASIC、通用型強勢出擊，2026 年美系 CSP 資本支出估年增 91%  
在 ChatGPT 等 AI 應用陸續推向市場，各大業者紛紛開始研發自有超級大模型，包括 Gemini 
(Google)、Claude (Anthropic) 、Llama (Meta) 等，各大品牌開始大舉投入大型語言模型(LLM)市場，
且每一個產品的智慧皆持續提高，部分消費者也陸續從 ChatGPT 陣營轉移至 Gemini 陣營，反映
出消費者開始逐漸提升對 AI 的黏著度。根據 Grand View Research 調查，2024 年全球 AI 市場營
收約 2,792 億美元，預估 2030 年市場規模超過 3 兆美元，擁有超過 30% 的年複合增長率。 
面對這爆發性的長期成長趨勢，CSP 業者(Amazon、Microsoft、Google、Meta)在法說會上一致強
調，AI 投資已轉變為一項橫跨 2026 年的結構性戰略，CSP 大廠不僅持續擴大對 GB300 等次世代
AI 伺服器的採購，更將重點放在資料中心基礎設施的長期戰略規劃，以應對 AI 運算對電力及散熱
系統強勁成長的需求，確保未來數年內擁有足夠的能源與容量來支持持續迭代的大模型部署。本中
心認為，2026 年美國四大 CSP 資本支出將年增 91%，且將有一半的成長來自於資料中心建置，代
表了資料中心的不足及未來伺服器需求。明年不僅僅是 NVIDIA GB300 的天下，AWS、Google、
Meta ASIC 的崛起也將是一大關鍵，其中又以 Google TPU 需求最為強勁，已經有兩個客戶預計將
採購 TPU。 
另外，受惠於 AI 訓練與推論需求爆發的連帶效應，本中心預估 2026 年通用型伺服器出貨量將年增
23%，其中以存儲型伺服器需求最為強勁。隨著生成式 AI 應用普及和使用者數量暴增，CSP 等業
者必須不斷擴充容量，且不僅限於高階 GPU 伺服器，龐大的 AI 模型訓練和推論在使用狀態中將消
耗大量的數據(DATA)，使得存儲型伺服器的需求大增。

ASIC 進入高速成長期，GPU 需求仍維持擴張 
從 AI 晶片預期出貨來看，GPU 與 ASIC 需求均持續成長，但 2026 年後 ASIC 成長動能明顯加速。
Nvidia GPU 出貨量預估由 2025 年 565.7 萬顆增至 2026 年 650 萬顆、2027 年 850 萬顆，NVIDIA
加 AMD GPU 預估年增 17%及 34%；AMD 亦由 2025 年 90 萬顆增至 2027 年 180 萬顆，顯示通用
GPU 算力需求仍維持成長。 
相較之下，ASIC 受惠 CSP 加速自研晶片及導入規模擴大，總出貨量預估由 2025 年 380 萬顆大幅
增至 2026 年 725 萬顆、2027 年 1,360 萬顆，年增率達 91%及 88%，成長速度明顯高於 GPU。其
中 Google 仍為最大 ASIC 使用者， 2027 年用量預估達 800 萬顆，AWS 亦增至 320 萬顆，
Microsoft 及 Meta 同步擴大部署。我們認為，在資料中心電力逐漸成為算力擴張瓶頸下， CSP 將
更重視每瓦運算效能及整體運算成本， ASIC 針對特定運算工作的效率優勢有望推升滲透率，惟
GPU 憑藉通用性及軟體生態系優勢，仍將維持重要地位。

圖 9：GPU/ASIC 出貨量預估

資料來源：元大投顧

AI 晶片面積持續擴大，將使 ABF 載板用量大增 
隨著大型語言模型與生成式 AI 的算力需求快速攀升，AI 晶片的設計持續朝向更高運算密度與更大
面積發展。無論是 NVIDIA、AMD 或各家大廠的新世代加速器，都呈現晶粒尺寸擴大、功耗上升、
I/O 腳位數倍增的趨勢。在此背景下，ABF 載板的需求不僅以「量」擴張，更以「規格」升級為主
軸。為支撐大型 GPU/AI ASIC 的高頻寬連接與多晶片架構，ABF 層數、線寬線距、孔徑控制及翹
曲管理均需同步提升，帶動高端載板技術門檻不斷拉高。由於先進 AI 晶片的封裝面積更大、電源
與訊號整合更複雜，單顆晶片所需 ABF 載板面積與材料用量明顯增加。整體而言，AI 晶片規模擴
大正驅動 ABF 產業進入新一輪升級周期，高階需求將成為未來成長的主要動能。

### Page 10

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：PCB 2026 年 10 月 5 日 Nan Ya PCB 第10 頁，共 19頁  
獲利調整與股票評價  
給予南電買進評等，目標價 1700 元 
本中心維持 2026–2027 年載板廠商將持續受惠於高稼動率與漲價趨勢的看法不變。主因新世代高
階載板規格持續大型化，傳統 PC／NB 載板尺寸多約 27×27mm，而 Server CPU 、AI ASIC 及高
速交 換器 載板 已逐 步 放大 至 70–80mm 、100mm 以 上， 部分 大型 產品 甚 至 朝 140×140mm 、
150×150mm 發展，未來更大型先進封裝架構更可能逼近 200mm 等級。由於載板生產係以固定尺
寸 Panel 進行製造，隨單顆載板面積放大，每張 Panel 可排入的產品數量將快速下降，同時邊角無
法有效利用所造成的 Area Loss 亦明顯增加，此將持續消耗載板廠商有效產能，有助產業維持高稼
動率，並支撐 2026–2027 年載板報價向上。本中心估計 2026/2027 年南電營收 599.8/1095.4 億元，
年增 49.3%/82.6%，毛利率 26.9%/43.4%，營業利益 141.3/442.3 億元，年增 612.7%/213.1%，
稅後獲利 117/366.9 億元，年增 501.2%/213.5%，EPS 18.11/56.78 元。 
綜合上述，南電高階產品比重提升、稼動率維持高檔及報價上調帶動下，預期 ABF／BT 毛利率可
望持續改善，整體營運動能於 2027 年明顯增強。評價方面，南電歷史本益比交易區間約介於 6–36
倍；考量本輪 ABF／BT 供需結構改善、高階產品組合升級，以及稼動率快速提升所帶來的毛利率
擴張，本中心給予 2027 年 30 倍本益比評價，以 2027 年預估 EPS 推算目標價 1,700 元，並給予
買進投資建議。

公司簡介  
南亞電路板成立於 1997 年，前身為南亞塑膠自 1985 年開始營運的電路板事業部，1998 年正式獨立營運，並於 2006 年在
台灣證券交易所掛牌上市，為台塑集團旗下主要 PCB 與 IC 載板廠商，目前南亞塑膠持股約 67%。南亞電路板     年報
_8046 南電_2025 年報(1) 公司產品涵蓋傳統多層印刷電路板、HDI、軟硬複合板（Rigid-Flex），以及 ABF 與 PP／BT IC
載板，生產據點主要位於台灣錦興、樹林及中國昆山。南亞電路板 其中 IC 載板為公司主要高階產品布局，應用涵蓋伺服器
CPU、AI ASIC、高效能運算（HPC）、800G／1.6T 高速交換器、記憶體、SSD 控制晶片、SiP、行動裝置及車用電子等
領域；一般 PCB 則持續往 AI 伺服器、GPU 加速卡、伺服器與高階網通等高附加價值產品升級。    年報_8046 南電_2025
年報(1) 公司近年營運重心進一步向 AI 與 HPC 相關高階載板移動，受惠 AI 晶片朝大尺寸、高層數及高 I/O 規格發展，以及
CSP 持續擴大自研 ASIC 布局，南電持續強化高階 ABF 載板、1.6T 交換器及 AI 伺服器相關產品布局，並規劃新增高階載
板產能，以支應下一階段 AI 基礎建設需求。

ESG 分析  
分別藉由「在 ESG 議題上的曝險」和「個別公司在 ESG 議題上的執行力」兩個面向來衡量公司於 ESG 上的表現。 
► ESG 總分： 南電整體的 ESG 風險評級屬於低等風險，於 Sustainalytics 資料庫所覆蓋的公司中排名亦排行於略為領先
的位置，在電子零組件行業的公司中排名略微領先同業。 
► 在 ESG 議題上的曝險： 南電的整體曝險屬於低等水準，略為領先電子零組件行業的平均水準。  
► 個別公司在 ESG 議題上的執行力：南電在針對重大 ESG 議題的管理水準和執行力屬中等。公司訊息揭露能力非常優良，
提供投資人及市場充足的資訊。

投資評等說明 
買進：根據本中心對該檔個股投資期間絕對或相對報酬率之預測，我們對該股持正面觀點。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股之發展前景、財務表現、利多題
材、評價資訊以及風險概況之分析。建議投資人於投資部位中增持該股。 
持有-超越同業：本中心認為根據目前股價，該檔個股基本面吸引力高於 同業。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股發展前景、財務表現、利多題材、評價資訊
以及風險概況之分析。 
持有-落後同業：本中心認為根據目前股價，該檔個股基本面吸引力低於 同業。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股發展前景、財務表現、利多題材、評價資訊
以及風險概況之分析。 
賣出：根據本中心對該檔個股投資期間絕對或相對報酬率之預測，我們對該股持負面觀點。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股之發展前景、財務表現、利多題
材、評價資訊以及風險概況之分析。建議投資人於投資部位中減持該股。 
評估中：本中心之預估、評等、目標價尚在評估中，但仍積極追蹤該個股。 
限制評等：為遵循相關法令規章及/或元大之政策，暫不給予評等及目標價。

註：元大給予個股之目標價係依 12 個月投資期間計算。大中華探索系列報告並無正式之 12 個月目標價，其投資建議乃根據分析師報告中之指定期間分析
而得。

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD: October 12-16
Date:    9 hours ago
ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD: October 12-16

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD:
October 12-16
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 11 Oct 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Monica Sauro / Unsplash

### Page 3

Last week was relatively quiet on the economic data front, but the
indicators released pointed to continued economic strength. ADP weekly
payroll gains increased for the fifth consecutive week, reaching a three-
month high, while jobless claims fell to multi-year lows. The ISM NM-PMI
remained solid at 54.9 in September, though its prices paid component
jumped to 74.0, the highest since July 2022. Redbook same-store sales
rose 8.3% y/y during the week of October 2, signaling continued strength

in consumer spending, while the New York Fed's consumer survey
showed expected spending growth and one-year-ahead inflation
expectations both reaching three-year highs.
The data suggest that the Fed has good reason to be hawkish.
Accordingly, September's FOMC meeting minutes showed that most
policymakers favored another rate hike by year-end, with several judging
monetary policy to be barely restrictive, if at all. We agree that further
tightening is warranted. However, recent comments by New York Fed

President John Williams, Fed Vice Chair Philip Jefferson, and Governor
Christopher Waller suggest flexibility on the timing. The markets assign a
17.2% probability to an October hike and a 71.6% probability of a
December hike.
This week's inflation reports could change those odds. A hotter-than-
expected CPI reading on Wednesday could prompt the markets to price in
an October hike, pressuring the Fed after September's unanimous 25bps
increase, which Fed Chair Kevin Warsh described as removing "a dose of

accommodation." An in-line or cooler CPI reading would support patience.
Warsh speaks Thursday evening at the IMF and World Bank annual
meetings in Bangkok.
Here's more:

### Page 4

(1) Inflation. The September CPI and PPI reports will be released on
Wednesday and Thursday, respectively. The Cleveland Fed’s Inflation
Nowcast model projects a hot 0.53% m/m increase in headline CPI, while
core CPI is expected to rise a more modest 0.2%. That would put headline
and core CPI inflation at 3.6% and 2.4% y/y, respectively. September is
expected to mark the 67th consecutive month of CPI inflation exceeding
2.0% y/y, underscoring the persistence of inflationary pressures (chart).

Adding to inflation concerns, the averages of the prices-paid and prices-
received indexes of five regional Fed banks’ business surveys rose to their
highest levels since 2022 in September (chart). Both have historically
tracked headline CPI inflation closely.

### Page 5

The same indicators have also closely tracked PPI final demand inflation,
which was already running hot at 5.4% y/y in August (chart).
(2) Monthly Treasury statement. The federal budget deficit totaled $1.77
trillion over the 12 months through August, while outstanding marketable
Treasury securities increased by $2.12 trillion over the 12 months through
September (chart). The recent slowdown in Treasury borrowing suggests
that the deficit may have narrowed somewhat. Tuesday's report will reveal

the final budget deficit for fiscal 2026, which ended on September 30.
(3) Consumer spending. Thursday's September retail sales report will
provide the first official reading on consumer spending for the month. We
expect another solid increase, as Redbook same-store sales rose 8.3%
y/y in September, well above the 2025 average of 5.8%. Redbook sales

### Page 6

historically have tracked official retail sales closely, suggesting that
consumer spending maintained strong momentum through the end of Q3
(chart).
(4) Manufacturing. The week will also provide important insights into the
manufacturing sector, with the October New York and Philadelphia Fed
banks’ business surveys due Thursday, followed by September's industrial
production and capacity utilization reports on Friday.
Manufacturing aggregate weekly hours rose to 84.4 in September, the

highest since August 2023, continuing their recent upward trend (chart).
Immediate expensing under the OBBBA, reshoring, and the AI buildout
are helping to reinvigorate manufacturing activity. With skilled labor in
short supply, manufacturers appear to be meeting stronger demand partly
by increasing the hours worked by existing employees. The rise in
aggregate hours worked suggests that manufacturing production
expanded at a healthy pace in September.

### Page 7

The New York and Philadelphia Fed manufacturing surveys provide
another reason for optimism. Their average manufacturing PMI rose to
22.7 in September, the highest since 2021 (chart). Historically, the
average has closely tracked manufacturing production growth, suggesting
that factory output could accelerate further in the months ahead.

Executive summary

即使最近我們觀察到部分 AI 伺服器產業鏈下游如 ODM 組裝廣達，滑軌川湖，
散熱系統雙鴻，及均熱⽚健策， 9 ⽉份訂單有明顯調整， 3Q26 營收⼩幅不如市
場預期，可能因為短期電⼒短缺及短料缺貨卡⻑料庫存調整，但 AI 數據算⼒中
⼼基礎建設的價格調整似乎還未停歇，先是有  Nebius 於 10/1 ⽇起全⾯調漲
AI GPU 租⽤價格達  17-21% ， CPU 達 25% ， Memory 達 41% （請參考之前
報告：17-21% On-demand AI GPU pricing hike to shift value from
LLM companies to cloud vendors），現在⼜有  AWS 於 10/7 ⽇起調漲  EC2
容量區塊牌價達 15% ，但使⽤客戶⼤語⾔模型公司  OpenAI, Anthropic 卻持
續降模型每百萬  Tokens 價格衝市佔，我們重申在電⼒短缺及短料缺貨卡⻑料
庫存造成既有算⼒稀缺的同時，短期市場價值將從賣  tokens 的⼤語⾔ / 專家

語⾔模型公司轉到  AI 數據中⼼算⼒基礎建設公司。

### Page 2

What’s new?
過去  24 ⼩時內，亞⾺遜雲端運算服務（ AWS ）官⽅更新了其  EC2 算⼒⽬
錄，正式⾃  2026 年  10 ⽉  7 ⽇起上調指定⾼階  GPU 預留容量區塊（ EC2
Capacity Blocks ）牌價，實質調漲幅度⾼達約  15% 。這項定價調整與亞⾺
遜正在推進的  80 億美元輝達  GPU 專項結構化融資案同步浮出⽔⾯。在此之
前，專⽤雲⿓頭  CoreWeave 與  Nebius 甫於  10 ⽉  1 ⽇將  H100 及  B200
現貨時租價格調漲  17% ⾄  21% 。 AWS 此次調價確認了公有雲⿓頭不再打價
格戰，⽽是全⾯跟進調價，彰顯出在企業⻑週期  Agent 與前線訓練負載湧⼊
下，頂級算⼒供給依舊供不應求。
付費訂閱戶可分享誰有利，誰受影響， Outside the
box 想法，建議，什麼是 EC2 capacity blocks

why?
超⼤規模公有雲巨頭：亞⾺遜（ Amazon AWS ）、微軟（ Microsoft

原因分析：預留算⼒價格上調  15% 將直接提振  AWS 雲端運算業務的平
均單元營收（ ARPU ）與營運利潤率，消除了市場對雲巨頭巨額  Capex

書，⽀撐了整體⾏業的⽑利擴張，我們相信其他公司也會陸續跟進。

### Page 3

Negatively aﬀect what supply chain vendors and
why?
預算受限的中⼩型  AI 開發者與微調新創。
尚未鎖定⻑期預留合約的⼆線軟體公司。
原因分析：算⼒租⾦成本上揚  15% 將直接壓縮⾮頭部  AI 應⽤公司的現
⾦跑道（ Runway ），迫使缺乏強勁變現能⼒的新創加速整合或退出市
場。
Our outside the box non-consensus thinking
市場直覺認為雲端巨頭提價可能引發客戶流失或轉向⾃建資料中⼼。然⽽我們
的⾮共識洞察是  AWS 此次提價並⾮單純的成本轉嫁，⽽是算⼒期貨化與優質
客戶篩選機制優化。在  EC2 Capacity Blocks 機制下，客戶鎖定的是「未來
特定時段確保可⽤的確定性算⼒」。在全美資料中⼼正⾯臨超過  100 項地⽅性
建置禁令（ Moratoriums ）與電網審批塞⾞的背景下，「即時可⽤的電⼒與算
⼒」成為絕對稀缺品。 AWS 藉由調價  15% ，有效過濾了投機性短期負載，將

關鍵算⼒優先供給具備⻑期訂閱付費能⼒的⾼價值企業客戶（如⽣成式醫療、
⾼頻⾦融與國防客戶），從⽽鎖定⻑達  3 ⾄  5 年的現⾦流可預測性。
Our advises and risks analysis
超配雲端巨頭⺟公司股權：AWS 提價確認了強⼤的定價權，亞⾺遜
（ AMZN ）預計將在 2027-2028 展現⾼於預期的利潤率修復。
持有  GPU 虛擬化與排程優化軟體股：隨著時租成本攀升，企業將加⼤採
購動態排程與⼯作負載優化軟體，推升相關供應商營收。
客戶架構優化導致  Token 效率提升：推論優化演算法可能減少總  GPU
租賃時⻑，這對雲巨頭是漲價後可能發⽣的⾵險。
總體經濟逆⾵壓抑  IT ⽀出：若企業削減  2027 年  IT 資本預算，可能影
響⾼價預留容量的續約率。

附註： EC2 及 EC2 capacity blocks 是什麼 ?
Amazon EC2 （ Elastic Compute Cloud ）是亞⾺遜雲端運算服務（ AWS ）
最核⼼的  IaaS （基礎設施即服務）產品，簡單說就是「在雲端租⽤的虛擬伺
服器」。企業與開發者不再需要⾃⾏採購、架設或維護實體機房與伺服器硬

### Page 4

體，只要透過網路，幾分鐘內就能按需開啟或關閉任意運算規格的主機，並依
實際使⽤時間計費。
⼀、  EC2 的核⼼概念與結構
1. 實例（ Instances ）：
EC2 中的虛擬機器統稱為「實例」。
AWS 依據不同的⼯作負載，將實例分成不同家族，例如：
通⽤型（ M 系列、 T 系列）：平衡  CPU 、記憶體與網路，常⽤
於⼀般網站、後端  API 。
運算優化型（ C 系列）：⾼單核效能，適⽤於批次處理、分散式
分析。
記憶體優化型（ R 系列、 X 系列）：配備海量  RAM ，⽤於記憶
體資料庫（ Redis/SAP HANA ）。
加速運算型（ P 系列、 G 系列、 Trn 系列）：搭載  NVIDIA ⾼階
GPU （如  H100 、 B200 、 B300 等）或  AWS ⾃研晶⽚
（ Trainium/Inferentia ），專⾨⽤於  AI ⼤模型訓練與推論。
2. 彈性（ Elastic ）：
資源可以⽔平擴展（ Auto Scaling ）或垂直升降級。例如電商遇到

購物節流量爆發，系統可⾃動從  5 台擴增⾄  50 台，活動結束後⾃
動銷毀，避免資源閒置浪費。
⼆、  常⾒的計費與購買模式
傳統上， EC2 的採購模式主要分為：
隨需（ On-Demand ）：隨開隨⽤、隨關隨停，按秒 / ⼩時收費，價格最
⾼但彈性最⼤。
節約計畫  / 預留（ Savings Plans / Reserved Instances ）：承諾
使⽤  1 年或  3 年，換取⾼達  40%–70% 的折扣。
競價實例（ Spot Instances ）：利⽤  AWS 當下閒置的閒置運算容量，
折扣極⾼，但隨時可能被  AWS 回收中斷。
三、  什麼是「 EC2 Capacity Blocks 」？
在  AI 浪潮爆發後，⾼階  GPU 供不應求。許多  AI 新創或研究團隊買不起
1~3 年的⻑約（ Reserved ），但⽤隨需（ On-Demand ）⼜常因機房沒卡⽽
啟動失敗，⽤  Spot 則可能在模型訓練到⼀半時被踢掉。
因此  AWS 推出了  EC2 Capacity Blocks for ML （機器學習容量區塊）：

運作機制：允許客戶提前預約未來某個特定⽇期、特定時段（最⻑可預約
數週前，租期由  1 天⾄數⼗天不等）的頂級  GPU 算⼒叢集（如  P5 、 P6

### Page 5

考，並⾮投資建議，個⼈應審慎評估⾵險，如因此造成任何損失，本⼈不負任
何擔保責任 ; We are here to provide our professional opinions on
company and industry analysis but not the investment advice. The

## Document 246: Genius Electronic(3406TW) Nomura 261005

Source PDF: all report pdf/Genius Electronic(3406TW) Nomura 261005.pdf
Report source: 未公開來源
Date: 2026-10-05
Pages: 16
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

EQUITY: TECHNOLOGY
Genius Electronic Optical   3406.TW 3406 TT
Global Markets Research
5 October 2026
EBO and CPO an ideal leverage of its capabilities 
Quantifying EBO and CPO opportunities; reiterate Buy
Action: Maintain Buy; raise TP to TWD1,415, implying ~34% upside
We expect AI optical interconnect and communication opportunities (e.g. co-packaged optics 
fiber array unit [CPO FAU], expanded beam optical [EBO], micro multi-fiber connector [MMC]) 
to transform the traditional optical lens industry over the next five years, and believe 
Taiwanese handset camera lens makers (e.g. Largan [3008 TT, Buy] and Genius) possess 
inherent advantages to cater to these new markets, given their high-precision molding+plastic 
injection, optical design, active alignment, optical coating and optical inspection capabilities, as 
well as clean-room facilities and sufficient free cash flow from cash-cow handset lens 
businesses.
In addition to holding the position of Apple’s (AAPL US, Not rated) No. 2 lens supplier, we believe 
Genius has obtained 3M’s (MMM US, Not rated) EBO ferrule order and will start shipping by end-
2026F or early-2027F. Genius appears well-positioned to supply various CPO FAU 
components (lens, prism, V groove, FA, etc.) and may potentially handle FAU assembly if 
requested by customers. We estimate the EBO and CPO-related new businesses will 
contribute <3% and 16-20% earnings for Genius in 2027F and 2028F, respectively (details 
in Fig. 10). Yet, given the high dollar content and long-term growth potential of CPO/FAU related 
products over the next five years, we believe optical lens makers with inherent advantages in this 
new field deserve stock re-rating; we thus raise our target P/E to 25x (near the high end of the 
historical range of 10-30x since 2020), from 17x. We reiterate Buy, and raise our TP to 
TWD1,415, based on 25x 2028F EPS of TWD56.61 (vs. prior TP of TWD568, based on 17x 
2025F EPS of TWD33.4, set two years ago). Genius trades at 25x 2027F P/E.
Apple: Sunny’s market share loss and continued camera upgrades in 2027-28F
Sunny (2382 HK, Neutral) earlier faced some yield issues with the new variable aperture (VA) 
main camera lens for the iPhone 18 Pro series, and this could benefit Largan (more directly) and 
Genius (indirectly) in the competitive landscape. It remains uncertain whether Genius will be 
introduced as a third lens supplier to iPhone Pro’ main camera in the future, and this poses an 
upside risk to our forecasts. However, given the headwinds of rising memory costs and the 
potential cannibalization of the 18 Pro series by the iPhone Duo, we forecast slower iPhone lens 
shipments from end-2026F to 1H27F, before the 20th anniversary iPhone (Pro Max) and the 
second-gen iPhone Duo are introduced in 2H27F. We expect further upgrades to the iPhone Pro 
main camera in 2H27F, and introduction of a new periscope camera for the Pro Max in 2H28F.

### Page 4

Optical communication – an ideal leverage of 
lens makers’ capabilities
Optical interconnect – from MPO to MMC and EBO
Multi-fiber push-on (MPO) is the current standardized physical-contact optical 
interconnect architecture designed to aggregate high-density ribbon fibers (typically 8, 12, 
16, 24 or 32 cores per ferrule) into a single compact interface. MPO connectors leverage 
precise physical contact enforced by a high mechanical spring force (around 10-20N) and 
steel guide pins for alignment to achieve ultra-low insertion loss (0.20-0.35dB) across 
clean fiber end-faces.
The micro multi-fiber connector (MMC) is another proprietary physical-contact optical 
interconnect standard beyond MPO developed by US Conec (unlisted) for high-density 
AI interconnects to directly address the server faceplate and panel space constraints. The 
MMC introduces a very small form-factor (VSFF) multi-fiber design that utilizes a 
miniaturized TMT ferrule (patented by US Conec; Fig. 1 ), and according to US Conec, 
the width of the TMT ferrule is similar to that of conventional MT ferrule yet with ~30% 
smaller in thickness and ~50% shorter in length to shrink the physical footprint to roughly 
one-third the size of an MPO. The spatial reduction consequently unlocks up to a 3x 
density advantage in patch panel ports and transceiver faceplates compared to standard 
MPO footprints (Fig. 2 ). Meanwhile, the MMC delivers a crucial density jump while 
preserving high optical performance, offering low insertion loss and supporting up to 
250um pitch multi-fiber ribbon arrays (including 12-, 16-, 24-, and 32-fiber configurations) 
to ensure interoperability with MPO ecosystems. The MMC also improves operational 
serviceability through US Conec’s DirectConec push-pull latching mechanism to allow 
technicians to plus and unplug individual cables even at a highly populated panel without 
external retraction tools.
Fig. 1:  TMT ferrule vs. conventional MT ferrule

Source: US Conec, Nomura research
Fig. 2:  MMC has a smaller footprint than MPO to enable a 3X 
optical interconnect density

Source: US Conec, Nomura research
Although MPO and MMC are the most popular standards of optical fiber connectors 
nowadays, they both rely on direct physical contact between ferrules and thus require 
higher spring pressure and precision alignments when connecting the cables. Also, 
physical contacts leave MPO and MMC’s exposed optical interfaces inherently 
susceptible to wear and tear over repeating mating cycles as well as potential signal 
degradation from dust and debris contamination (and therefore needs frequent cleaning 
and inspection).
Expanded beam optical (EBO), a patented technology by 3M (MMM US, Not rated; 3M 
owns over 100 patents on it, with 50+ patents pending), reshapes optical interconnect 
technologies by addressing pre-mentioned challenges of MPO or MMC, since EBO 
replaces traditional physical-contact end-face matings with non-contact, lens-based 
beam expansion (Fig. 4 ). EBO utilizes integrated total internal reflection (TIR) mirrors 
within precision polymer ferrules. As the light exits an optical fiber core and enters the 
ferrule, it strikes the internal TIR mirror that collimates and expands light beams up to 10x 
their core diameter before travelling through an intentional, tiny air gap to the mating 
Nomura | Genius Electronic Optical 5 October 2026
4

### Page 5

connector. An identical, paired TIR mirror is integrated within the receiving ferrule to 
capture the expanded beam and focuses it back into the receiving fiber core. According to 
3M, the EBO ferrule is vapor-coated with a special anti-reflection coating that efficiently 
reduces the reflection of light beams (Fig. 5 ).
Fig. 3:  An EBO ferrule

Source: 3M, Nomura research
Fig. 4:  Connection of two EBO ferrules

Source: Rosenberger, Nomura research

Fig. 5:  A cross-section illustration of EBO

Source: 3M, EBO MSA, Nomura research

Although one of the underlying issues with EBO is a higher insertion loss (0.35-0.75dB), 
3M’s EBO connector system still provides a few advantages compared to traditional 
physical-contact connectors:
Physical-contact connectors require frequent inspection and cleaning cycle before 
mating, while EBO is nearly immune to airborne particle contamination thanks to the 
embedded lens system and no direct surface contacts. An easier handling with 
reduced maintenance efforts therefore significantly lowers the installation time (as 
much as by 85%, according to 3M) and labor cost.
•
EBO can ensure consistent signal integrity across thousands of mating cycles due 
to contact-less designs to avoid face-wear and micro-scratching. By contrast, physical 
connectors grind against each other and ultimately lacks long-term durability.
•
Traditional connectors require precise fiber-to-fiber alignments, whereas the EBO 
connector system can be used in a hermaphroditic condition (socket/connector 
agnostic) given identical connector at both ends. As such, the risk of installation 
error is largely eliminated, even without the need for fiber-optic specialists.
•
EBO only demand a fraction (e.g. one-tenth) of the high spring pressure that 
compresses glass end-faces together in physical connectors, thereby minimizing 
•
Nomura | Genius Electronic Optical 5 October 2026
5

### Page 6

mechanical strains on chassis frames (demo video by 3M ). 
The contact-less, high density, blind mating, and low spring pressure features 
make EBO potentially an ideal optical connector for optical back-plane interconnect 
applications (similar to the function of orthogonal backplane or mid-plane in nVidia’s 
[NVDA US, Not rated] Kyber architecture), in our view.  
•
Fig. 6:  3M's current EBO ferrule offerings (12F/16F)

Source: 3M, Nomura research

The EBO Multi-Source Agreement (EBO MSA) was established in March 2026 to turn 
3M’s proprietary EBO technology into an open, industry-wide standard for AI cluster 
infrastructure. The primary objective is to standardize EBO connector mechanical form 
factors, latching mechanisms, and optical fiber interface dimensions to allow hyperscalers 
to source interchangeable EBO connectors from multiple vendors rather than relying on 
one single supplier. According to EBO MSA, there are 9 end-user companies (including 
Microsoft [MSFT US, Not rated], Meta [META US, Not rated], nVidia, AMD [AMD US, Not 
rated], etc.) and 48 vendors in the membership by the end of September 2026 (Fig. 7 ). 
Members vote on specific connectors to develop and contribute design specifications – 
there are 18 types under discussion, and four are on the roadmap:
128f (8x16f) design specification is underway.•
16f MPO design is under discussion on specification.•
16f VSFF and external laser small form factor pluggable (ELSFP) are planned, and 
we note that EBO development for ELSFP could be challenging since high-power 
laser could damage the AR coating and polymer ferrules.
•

Who are the potential adopters of EBO connectors?
Of course, all the key members of EBO MSA are potential users of EBO connectors. But 
Nomura | Genius Electronic Optical 5 October 2026
6

### Page 7

for more concrete industry developments, 3M and Microsoft announced strategic
partnership in July 2026 (link ), and Microsoft was the first announced hyperscale cloud
provider to deploy 3M EBO technology in its Azure cloud. Microsoft’s early use of EBO
technology has shown the potential to reduce network deployment timelines in certain
environments. The technology has also demonstrated strong signal performance in live
data center conditions, where dust exposure and routine handling are typical during

installation and maintenance. The technology will help Microsoft reduce the need for
frequent cleaning and inspection while supporting reliable optical performance in dense,
high-volume deployment environments.
Meta joined EBO MSA in May 2026, and from our industry checks, we think Meta is likely
evaluating EBO technology for future ASIC mass-volume optical interconnect demand.
We also observe that AWS (AMZN US, Not rated), although it is not yet an MSA member,

could potentially evaluate this technology for its future ASIC mass-volume optical
interconnect demand.
Why optical plastic lens companies have advantages in
producing ferrules for passive optical connectors?
From MPO to MMC, the fiber density increases to 3x, and we notice that the precision
level requirements for MMC TMT ferrules also increase substantially versus MPO MT
ferrules. The high precision levels (<5um, or even <3um) of MMC TMT ferrules

usually exceed the traditional optical connector makers’ plastic injection capability
(usually >10um) and traditional connector makers need to invest new, higher precision
level tools to deal with those higher precision requirements. However, those precision
levels are about the right levels (or even lower-end than) which plastic camera lens
makers can do without much new investment in machine tools. This is why we think
plastic lens makers have advantage in the advanced passive optical connectors. The

reason why we do not see many lens makers enter MMC ferrule market at this moment is
because it is a patented technology by US Conec, and US Conec only licenses to limited
suppliers, such as Sumitomo (5802 JP, Buy), etc.
For EBO as well, although EBO ferrule looks complex in design and shape, it is a suitable
product for plastic camera lens makers to do. We estimate the difficulty and price of
EBO ferrule is around the levels of mid-range handset camera lenses.

3M likely outsources EBO ferrule to Genius
From our industry survey, 3M will likely outsource the EBO ferrule components to Genius,
which is done by high-precision plastic injection. Genius can leverage its handset camera
lens equipment and make new molds to produce the product. Hence, we do not expect
too much extra capex is required.
In our understanding, fiber connector makers can produce EBO after getting authorized
by 3M. We suspect that 3M will supply the key EBO ferrule to the authorized

manufacturers at least in the early stage of technology development. This may explain
why 3M comes to reach handset lens makers for the ferrule production.
Our base assumption for Genius’ EBO ferrule revenue contribution is about 2-3%
for 2027F and 2028F, by assuming an ASP of USD0.5-1 with 20-35mn units annual
volume. We assume the GM for this new product to be higher than corporate average.
The upside of EBO market will depend on the adoption and penetration of the technology

into hyperscalers’ AI optical interconnect roadmap. By our estimate, every rack
(containing 60-80 units of GPUs or AI ASICs), if adopting EBO for optical scale-up for
intra-rack or cross-rack interconnect technology, will potentially require 600-1,000
units of EBO ferrule components (300-500 pairs). Our base volume assumption for
Genius’ EBO shipments require one major CSP to adopt EBO in one major AI
platform, plus global sampling, yield loss, trial run demand and necessary

inventory buffer. If there are more than one major AI projects adopt EBO in 2028F, it will
represent potential upside to our forecasts.
CPO opportunities could be bigger than EBO, but less clear at
this moment
Unlike that EBO ferrule is a confirmed new business for Genius (though the market TAM
upside is unclear at this moment), CPO opportunities to Genius remain uncertain in
details, such as what products to offer, what are the business models, and who are

intermediate customers and/or end customers. Not to mention, the ramp-up of CPO
Nomura | Genius Electronic Optical 5 October 2026
7

### Page 8

market itself also has high uncertainties.
We believe Genius is working with FOCI (3363 TT, Not rated) in supplying key 
components for CPO FAU, such as V groove, prism, and/or fiber array (FA). It is also 
likely in talk with other FAU makers for various component supply (including lens, V 
groove, prism, FA, etc.). These FAU makers tend to do FAU final assembly in-house, and 
hence, may treat Genius as a component supplier to fill its internal capability or 
outsourcing partners’ gap. Genius’ flexibility in various component offering for CPO FAU 
could make it an easier partner for FAU customers, versus Largan (it tends to do more FA 
or FAU, rather than just components), and may see revenue contribution earlier than 
Largan. However, we assume USD25-35 ASP for Genius’ CPO FAU component offering 
in our base assumptions, given that it may supply more components rather than doing 
finished FAU products (ASP USD>100).
In the other side, we also see opportunities for Genius to get FAU module business from 
customers like Corning (GLW US, Not rated), but the progress of Genius to do such full 
assembly lines could be slower than Largan, as Genius still relies more on external 
equipment supply, unlike Largan having strong internal equipment design and 
manufacturing capability.
We assume CPO FAU to contribute Genius’ EPS by <1% in 2027F and 17% in 2028F.

Definition of Nomura Group's equity research rating system and sectors

valuation methodology determined by the analyst. Valuation methodologies include, but are not limited to, discounted cash flow analysis, expected 
return on equity and multiple analysis. Analysts may also indicate expected absolute upside/downside relative to the stated target price, defined as

SECTORS
A 'Bullish' stance, indicates that the analyst expects the sector to outperform the Benchmark during the next 12 months. A 'Neutral' stance, 
indicates that the analyst expects the sector to perform in line with the Benchmark during the next 12 months. A 'Bearish' stance, indicates that the 
analyst expects the sector to underperform the Benchmark during the next 12 months. Sectors that are labelled as 'Not rated' or shown as 'N/A' are 
not assigned ratings. Benchmarks are as follows: United States: S&P 500; Europe: Dow Jones STOXX 600; Global Emerging Markets (ex-Asia): 
MSCI Emerging Markets ex-Asia. Japan/Asia ex-Japan: Sector ratings are not assigned.

Target Price
A Target Price, if discussed, indicates the analyst’s forecast for the share price with a 12-month time horizon, reflecting in part the analyst's 
estimates for the company's earnings. The achievement of any target price may be impeded by general market and macroeconomic trends, and by 
other risks related to the company or the market, and may not occur if the company's earnings differ from estimates.

Style Exposure
We assume coverage of Jentech at OW with a Dec-27 PT at NT$7,500, based on
30x 2028E P/E. We are positive on the company’s near-term and long-term
earnings drivers including: (1) the adoption schedule of the 3-piece / removable lid
design (removable lid + stiffener + multiple screws) could accelerate to 4Q26 with
small production in Rubin GPU and mass production from 1Q27 for Rubin/Rubin

Ultra, lifting lid content ~4–5x versus the current 1-piece design; (2) AMD MI450-
series moves to a 2-piece lid from 4Q26, driving ~10x content versus MI350; (3)
longer-cycle upside comes from CPO/NPO ramps with higher stiffener content;
and (4) potential ASIC upgrades to higher-spec solutions. As Jentech’s share price
has rallied 108% in the past three months (vs. TWSE index +102%), we believe the
above-mentioned positive factors have started to be reflected in the share price,

making our PT upside less attractive (~10%) vs. other cooling component names.
• No delay in 3-piece/removable lid adoption in Rubin Ultra, even pulling
forward some volume in Rubin potentially from 4Q26/1Q27. We continue
to believe Nvidia’s Rubin Ultra GPU will adopt the “removable lid + stiffener
+ multiple screws” design (or the so-called 3-piece lid design) with no delay
or change, which should drive the heat spreader/lid content increase by 4-5x vs.

the current 1-piece lid design for Rubin GPU. Based on our industry checks,
Nvidia might even adopt this new design in small volumes of Rubin GPU
potentially from 4Q26 for trial production, in order to ensure decent yield when
entering mass production in the next gen (potentially in 1Q27). We believe the
adoption schedule is slightly ahead of market expectations, which might
provide near-term revenue/earnings upside for the 3-piece lid supplier.
• Solid 3Q26 and more growth into 4Q26 and 1Q27 with MI450 2-piece lid

ramp and initial removable lid contribution for Rubin/Rubin Ultra.  We
estimate our 3Q26 revenue at NT$9.7bn (+33% QoQ and +91% YoY) with the
decent ramp of Rubin GPU. As the shipment of Rubin 1-piece lid should
remain at high volume into 4Q26, we believe the company’s revenue
momentum will also continue, with incremental growth from the mass
production of AMD MI450 2-piece lid with ~10x content increase (vs. MI350
series). Hence, we are now modeling Jentech’s 4Q26 revenue to further grow

7% QoQ. In addition, if the 3-piece/removable lid design could be adopted in
small volume of Rubin GPU from 4Q26 and enter mass production from 1Q27
in Rubin Ultra GPU, we believe there could be further upside to the company’s
growth magnitude in the next few quarters.
• Valuation re-rating already kicking off. Jentech is now trading at ~50x 12M
fwd P/E, at the high-end of its historical valuation range, but from the 2028E
EPS-based perspective, it is only trading at 25-30x P/E. With a longer-term

Summary Investment Thesis and Valuation
Investment Thesis
Jentech (OW) is the largest packaging-level cooling component
maker worldwide, with leading production capabilities across
heat spreader products (including stiffener and all kinds of lids).
We expect the company to show an ~80% EPS CAGR over
2024-28, driven by solid heat spreader content growth in future-

gen AI chips and increasing TAM into new solutions (e.g., CPO
switch ASIC). In addition, the company is reinforcing its
product portfolio with new offerings in cold plate and CPU
server sockets for chip customers. We believe Jentech’s
technology leadership in the structural cooling upgrade trend
and its new TAM expansion will continue to support its superior
earnings power in the long term.
Valuation
Our Dec-27 PT of NT$7,500 is based on 30x 2028E P/E, which

is largely in line with Jentech’s three-year average P/E when the
AI server boom started.

Earnings estimates
JPM’s above-consensus forecasts
Our 2026E/27E/28E EPS forecasts are 1%/3%/6% above Bloomberg consensus,
reflecting 1) faster adoption of the removable lid design in 4Q26/1Q27 for Nvidia
Rubin/Rubin Ultra GPU with 4-5x content increase vs current 1-piece lid, 2) better heat
spreader spec upgrades in future GPUs and ASICs, and 3) more meaningful CPO/NPO

ramp with higher-spec solutions being adopted.

Key risks to our estimates
• Potential delay in mainstream 3-piece/removable lid design adoption: As the
spec/TDP of Nvidia’s Rubin Ultra GPU has not yet been finalized, there is still risk
of the 3-piece/removable lid adoption if the chip spec improvement turns out to be
limited. However, based on our recent industry checks, Nvidia might even adopt this
new design in small volume of Rubin GPU potentially from 4Q26 for trial
production, in order to ensure decent yield when entering mass production in the

next gen (potentially in 1Q27), which would lower the risk.
• Market share loss from competition: Based on our supply chain checks, another
Taiwanese heat spreader supplier, I-Chiun (2486 TT) has secured some CPU and
ASIC stiffener projects. However, in terms of the AI GPU lid and other higher-spec
projects (e.g CPO ASIC stiffeners), we think there is a huge gap between Jentech
and other suppliers.

Performance and valuation
Share price performance
Figure 1: Jentech’s share price vs Taiex
Source: Bloomberg Finance L.P.
• In 2025, Jentech’s share price underperformed in 1H given the market concerns of
an AI bubble and potential heat spreader de-specing trend (from lid to stiffener).
However, the share price outperformed from 2H given the new high-spec heat

spreader designs (such as micro-channel lid, 2-piece lid, etc) potentially for Nvidia’s
future AI GPUs to enable TDP migration.
• In 1Q26, Jentech’s share price continued outperforming the Taiex in the beginning
with new TAM expansion opportunities from Nvidia’s new solutions, especially the
CPO related ones (e.g. Spectrum and Quantum ASICs). From the mid of 2Q26, the
share price corrected meaningfully owing to the Rubin GPU design change from 2-

piece lid back to 1-piece lid with only ⅓ of ASP.
• In 3Q26, Jentech’s share price was still weak in the beginning among the overall
market pullback from de-leveraging. However, it has rebounded rapidly from
August thanks to 1) reported GM/EPS numbers beat, 2) smooth Rubin 1-piece lid
ramp with ASP upside, and 3) more meaningful content value growth for Rubin
Ultra GPU with 4-5x ASP vs the current 1-piece lid.

Financial analysis
We forecast Jentech’s revenue to increase 61%/95%/59% YoY in 2026E/27E/28E,
supported by the removable lid design for Nvidia Rubin/Rubin Ultra GPU with 4-5x
content increase (vs current 1-piece lid), with continued heat spreader spec upgrades in
future GPUs and ASICs. Also, the more meaningful CPO/NPO ramp with higher-spec

solutions adopted also contributed to the solid growth.
Jentech’s 2Q26 gross margin expanded to 47% thanks to continued QD yield
enhancement. We believe the company’s margin will continue to surprise to the upside
in 2H26-2028, driven by the favorable product mix from stronger AI server product
shipments, which should support our gross margin forecast of 47+%/50%/52% in
2H26E/2027E/2028E. The solid GM growth also serves as the key driver of our bottom-

line EPS growth of 89%/117%/68% in 2026E/27E/28E.
